### `c3.4-per-source-rate-limit`

- **Story:** 1.13 (Per-source rate-limit circuit breaker).
- **Merge SHA:** `<merge-sha>` (PR `<pr-number>`, merged `<merge-date>` by `olokotoh`). Branch `epic-1/story-1-13-per-source-rate-limit-circuit-breaker` carries the implementation; post-merge backfill commit `chore: backfill Story 1.13 merge SHA in traceability matrix` updates this row.
- **PR:** `<pr-number>`.
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR51 (partially satisfied; the sensing-layer half lands here as `internal/ratelimit/`, applied at each adapter's pre-publish path. The LLM-tier half lands in a future Story 3.x at `internal/decision/agent/circuitbreaker.go` per `architecture.md:966`). NFR22 (satisfied; the breaker engages at 1000 events/sec/source/node by default with 10% deterministic hash sampling, surfaces a Prometheus counter, hot-reloads via the config.Manager.Subscribe callback). NFR23 (informed; the LLM-tier breaker shares this metric naming convention and registry-isolation pattern). FR49 (informed; the four knobs hot-reload through the existing watcher infrastructure via the Subscribe callback wired in `cmd/olaitan/main.go`). NFR32 (satisfied; cardinality stays at 5 sources x 1 node-per-pod = 5 series per pod by registering `node` as a const-label set from `K8S_NODE_NAME` at metric-construction time). NFR35 (informed; the unit + integration tests on `internal/ratelimit/` cover the publish hot path, the engage/disengage transitions under fake and real clocks, the sampling-fraction chi-squared bound, and the hot-reload contract end-to-end). NFR36 (satisfied; integration tests drive a real `*Limiter`, a real `prometheus.Registry`, a real `httptest.NewServer`, and a real `config.Manager` watching a real file -- no mock substitutions of the rate-limit primitive or the metric registry). NFR42 (satisfied; sampling decisions use FNV-1a hash of `event.ID` so two evaluation re-runs against the same event sequence yield identical sample/drop verdicts, locked in by `TestAllow_SamplingDeterministic`).
- **Architecture reconciliation:** `architecture.md:966` maps FR51 to `internal/correlator/trigger/sampler.go` plus `internal/decision/agent/circuitbreaker.go`. The first half is reinterpreted as `internal/ratelimit/` rather than the correlator path because (a) the breaker is applied at the SENSING boundary on each adapter's publish path, not at the correlator's trigger boundary; (b) `internal/correlator/` is currently only a `.gitkeep` placeholder for Story 1.14 -- putting a sensing primitive inside a not-yet-existing correlator package would invert the dependency direction (sensing imports correlator); (c) Story 1.14 may add a separate correlator-side sampler for fan-out concerns; that separate primitive can live at the architecture's stated path and consume `internal/ratelimit/` if needed. The LLM-tier breaker (`internal/decision/agent/circuitbreaker.go`) is out of scope here and lands in a future Story 3.x.
- **Schema additivity:** `Sampled bool` and `SamplingRate float64` are appended to `internal/schema/event.go` with `json:"sampled,omitempty"` and `json:"sampling_rate,omitempty"` tags. An unsampled event marshals to a byte-identical wire form against the pre-1.13 schema, so the EVENTS.raw `MaxMsgSize` budget (256 KiB per Story 1.9) is unaffected. The `docs/schema-versioning.md` ADR records this as a MINOR version bump per the project's semver convention.
- **Sliding-window measurement:** the limiter measures rate over a 1-second sliding window via a 10-bucket ring (100ms per bucket). Per-`Allow` cost is O(1); no per-instance goroutine is spawned; engage/disengage is evaluated lazily on the hot path (guardrail 30). The ring is protected by a `sync.Mutex` held only over the bucket-increment + transition-evaluation arithmetic; the `OnTransition` callback fires after the mutex is released so subscribers cannot deadlock by calling back into the limiter.
- **Three new guardrails (28-30):** (28) deterministic sampling via stable event-ID hash -- the decision uses `FNV-1a(event.ID) mod 100 < samplingRate*100`, not `math/rand`, so Epic 5 evaluation re-runs are reproducible against the same event sequence; (29) engage/disengage transitions log once -- sustained engagement does NOT emit periodic logs because that would re-amplify the log-volume problem the breaker mitigates; (30) cooldown re-evaluates lazily on the next Allow call -- no per-(source, node) timer goroutine is spawned, eliminating goroutine-leak risk and keeping the limiter at zero idle goroutines.
- **Per-adapter wiring:** every streaming adapter (falco/audit/cri/cni/applog) gains a `RateLimit *ratelimit.Limiter` field on its Config, a `*ratelimit.Limiter` field on the Adapter struct (always non-nil after `New` via a disabled-fallback), an `EngagedTotal() int64` getter delegating to `Limiter.EngagedTotal()`, a `DroppedBySampling() int64` getter for the future Story 1.18 metric binding, and a `Limiter() *ratelimit.Limiter` accessor so the hot-reload Subscribe callback can call `UpdateThreshold/Cooldown/SamplingRate/Enabled` without a process restart. The pre-publish path on each adapter calls `Allow(ev.ID)` and either drops the event, annotates the event with `Sampled=true`+`SamplingRate`, or publishes unmodified depending on the engagement state.
- **Metrics binding:** `olaitan_sensor_circuit_breaker_engaged_total{source, node}` is registered in `cmd/olaitan/metrics.go registerAdapterCounters` for every streaming adapter via the existing `metrics.Registry.RegisterCounter` API. The `node` const-label is sourced from `K8S_NODE_NAME` at metric-registration time, which keeps cardinality bounded under NFR32 (5 sources x 1 node-per-pod = 5 series per pod). The `adapterMetrics` structural-typed interface gains `EngagedTotal() int64` so a new streaming adapter inherits the wiring without amending the per-adapter switch.
- **Helm wiring:** the chart introduces a top-level `rateLimit:` values block with four knobs (`enabled`, `thresholdEventsPerSec`, `cooldownSeconds`, `samplingRate`). The `configmap.yaml` bridge propagates all four knobs into the rendered `olaitan.yaml` via the Story 1.10 P28 `mustRegexFind + regexReplaceAll` pattern, with per-knob anchor checks so a missing anchor fails render with a clear message rather than silently dropping the operator's `--set`. Three new helm tests (`TestRateLimitDefaultsRendered`, `TestRateLimitBridgeRendersThreshold`, `TestRateLimitDisabledRendered`) lock the chart contract in.
- **Hot-reload contract (FR49):** `startCollectorRing` accepts a `subscribe func(func(*config.Config))` parameter that the runtime wires to `mgr.Subscribe`. The callback inspects `newCfg.RateLimit` and calls `UpdateThreshold/Cooldown/SamplingRate/Enabled` on every per-adapter limiter, so a `helm upgrade --set rateLimit.thresholdEventsPerSec=500` propagates within the watcher's debounce window without a process restart. `TestIntegration_RateLimitHotReloadViaManager` exercises this end-to-end against a real file rewrite + fsnotify watcher.
- **Hand-offs:**
  - **Story 1.14 (correlator and EvidencePackage assembly)** will consume `schema.Event.Sampled` in its trigger logic per FR12: sampled events should be deprioritised under EvidencePackage 128 KiB overflow (rule matches first, then baseline deviations, then severity >=50 events, then summarisation; sampled events are summarisation candidates not full-fidelity carriers).
  - **Story 1.18 (deterministic detection observability)** will register dashboards reading `olaitan_sensor_circuit_breaker_engaged_total{source, node}` as the primary back-pressure signal. The expected alert is "any sustained engagement >5 minutes" (`increase(...[5m]) >= 1` for 5 minutes) indicating a workload or attack source the operator should investigate. The same story will bind `DroppedBySampling` to a Prometheus counter so dashboards can show the drop volume.
  - **Story 3.x (LLM-tier circuit breaker)** will register `olaitan_decision_circuit_breaker_engaged_total{tier="llm"}` via the same `metrics.Registry.RegisterCounter` API. The LLM-tier breaker is global (not per-source per-node) and the threshold is 10 LLM-eligible packages per minute per NFR23. The implementation lives in `internal/decision/agent/circuitbreaker.go` per `architecture.md:966`.
  - **Story 4.x (DFIR forensic report writer)** will surface the `Sampled` flag in the report's evidence-fidelity section: a forensic report covering a sampled period must honestly disclose "10 percent of source X's events captured during 14:23:04 -- 14:24:17" rather than implying full-fidelity coverage. This is the FR45-adjacent integrity requirement.
- **External dependency:** no new external dependencies. `hash/fnv`, `sync`, `sync/atomic`, and `time` are all stdlib.
- **ADRs:** none. The binding interpretations above resolve the AC-vs-codebase asymmetries inline. The schema additivity is recorded in `docs/schema-versioning.md`.
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** infrastructure substrate; the rate limiter's engagement during high-volume attacks is observed via metric counters in Epic 5 evaluation runs but is not gate-checked. Production evaluation lands with the Epic 5 scenario runs once Stories 1.18 and 5.6 wire the dashboards and FR55 trials.

Histogram bucket boundaries land with Stories 1.13/1.14/1.18.
- **Two-server lifecycle:** `internal/metrics.Server` is a separate `http.Server` from `internal/health.Server`. The package docstring on `internal/metrics/metrics.go` documents the rationale: kubelet's probe SLO and Prometheus' scrape SLO have different consumer expectations, and bundling them on one port would force kubelet to inherit scrape latency. The two servers run in parallel under the same errgroup; `TestIntegration_DualServerGracefulShutdown` locks in the no-deadlock invariant on ctx-cancel within the 5s `shutdownTimeout` budget per server.
- **Registry isolation (guardrail 25):** Story 1.12 never touches `prometheus.DefaultRegisterer`. Every `metrics.Registry` is constructed via `prometheus.NewRegistry()` so tests are order-independent and future surfaces (debug exporters, sidecar exporters) can land without colliding on metric names. The same isolation pattern Stories 1.7 and 1.8 used for the `httptest.NewServer` substrate.
- **Reader-side counters (guardrail 26):** every per-adapter counter is registered via `prometheus.NewCounterFunc` reading from the existing adapter-side atomic (`eventsPublished`, `translateErrors`, `publishDrops`, `oversizeDropped`, `consecutiveEOFs`, `rejected`). The metrics layer never owns a writeable counter that the adapter increments; the adapter's atomic remains the single source of truth and there is no duplicate state to synchronise. Locked in by `TestRegisterAdapter_ConcurrentIncrementsNoDataRace` (race detector + 1M concurrent increments + concurrent scrapes).
- **Mandatory endpoint (guardrail 27):** `config.Validate` rejects an empty `metrics.address`; the FR50 surface is not optional. `Load` substitutes the package default `:9090` when the operator omits the block (chart-deploy compatibility); operators who explicitly type `metrics.address: ""` get the rejection at `Load` time. Locked in by `TestMetricsConfigValidateRejectsEmptyAddress` and `TestMetricsConfigOmittedSubstitutesDefault`.
- **Posture binding interpretation:** posture is the sixth Ring 1 source per `architecture.md:45` but it is read-on-demand, not streaming. It does NOT expose `source_healthy` (no continuous connection to lose; "healthy" is ambiguous for a request-response client) and does NOT expose `olaitan_sensor_events_total` (does not publish to `EVENTS.raw`). Instead it exposes the six counters from Story 1.11 (`PostureCacheHits`, `PostureCacheMisses`, `PostureCacheBypasses`, `PostureAPIErrors`, `PostureOrphanPods`, `PostureUnavailable`) as `olaitan_sensor_posture_*_total`. When `posture.enabled=false`, a single `olaitan_sensor_posture_disabled` gauge (value 1.0) is registered instead so dashboards distinguish "intentionally off" from "broken".
- **Helm wiring:** the chart introduces a top-level `metrics:` values block with three knobs (`address`, `containerPort`, `scrapeAnnotations`). The DaemonSet and Deployment templates gain a `metrics` named containerPort (`9090` by default) and three `prometheus.io/{scrape,port,path}` pod annotations gated by `scrapeAnnotations`. The `configmap.yaml` bridge propagates `metrics.address` into the rendered `olaitan.yaml` via the Story 1.10 P28 `mustRegexFind + regexReplaceAll` pattern; four new helm tests (`TestMetricsContainerPortPresent`, `TestMetricsAnnotationsPresentByDefault`, `TestMetricsAnnotationsAbsentWhenDisabled`, `TestMetricsBridgeRendersAddress`) lock the chart contract in. A `ServiceMonitor` resource is left as a future Prometheus-Operator-aware deployment follow-up tracked in `deferred-work.md`; a `:9090` ingress NetworkPolicy template lands with the cluster-wide policy story in Epic 6.
- **Hand-offs:**
  - **Story 1.13 (rate-limit circuit breaker)** will register `olaitan_sensor_circuit_breaker_engaged_total{source,node}` via the same `metrics.Registry.RegisterCounter` API; the `source` label values match the five-source enum landed here.
  - **Story 1.14 (correlator)** will register `olaitan_correlator_trigger_to_publish_seconds` (histogram, bucket boundaries match the 60s sliding-window + 100ms NFR2 ceiling envelope) and `olaitan_correlator_evidence_packages_total{trigger}` via the same Registry.
  - **Story 1.18 (deterministic detection observability)** will register the rule-engine and baseline-engine surfaces under the same naming convention (`olaitan_<ring>_<metric>{labels}`) and registry-isolation pattern.
  - **Story 3.15 (LLM observability)** will register `olaitan_llm_*` metrics with a `provider` label per NFR32 cardinality bounds.
- **External dependency:** `github.com/prometheus/client_golang v1.23.2` was already present as an indirect dep (pulled in via the controller-runtime dependency graph from Story 1.11). Story 1.12 promotes it to a direct dep via the explicit import.
- **ADRs:** none. The metric naming convention (`olaitan_<ring>_<metric>{labels}`) lives in `architecture.md:374`; the binding interpretations above resolve the AC-vs-codebase asymmetries inline.
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** infrastructure substrate; production evaluation in Epic 5 once the detection surfaces (Stories 1.18, 3.15) populate the metric families.

### `c3.4-workload-posture-client`

- **Story:** 1.11 (On-demand workload posture client).
- **Merge SHA:** `98a643a` (PR #20, merged 2026-05-13). Story 1.11 round-1 follow-up commit `2a03d2c` ("Story 1.11 follow-up: address Copilot + bmad-code-review on PR #20") applied 20+ patches (8 Copilot + 12 reviewer findings across Blind Hunter / Edge Case Hunter / Acceptance Auditor layers) on top of the main commit `6207c0e`; round-2 follow-up commit `a7959c1` ("Story 1.11 follow-up: address round-2 Copilot review on PR #20") applied 7 additional Copilot patches. Both follow-up commits are covered by this row.
- **PR:** #20 (merged 2026-05-13).
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR7 (satisfied; `internal/collector/posture/posture.Client` returns a `*schema.WorkloadPosture` populated with RoleBindings, ClusterRoleBindings, applicable NetworkPolicies, pod-level PodSecurityContext, and per-container SecurityContext summaries from a single synchronous K8s-API fan-out per `Get`). FR11 (informed; the posture struct travels inside `EvidencePackage.workload_posture` at correlator assembly time; Story 1.14 wires the consumer). FR13 (informed; the owner-resolver walks `Pod → ReplicaSet → Deployment` or `Pod → Job → CronJob` at most two K8s API hops per architecture's read-on-demand cost budget, with orphan-pod fallback to `<namespace>/Pod/<pod-name>` per `architecture.md:457`. Story 1.10's `pod_name_kind:generatename` tag is the upstream hint consumed by the correlator before calling `Get`). NFR10 (informed; the in-memory TTL cache is unbounded by entry count under the O(100)-workloads design assumption; at ~2 KiB per posture struct the cache costs ~200 KiB inside the 1 GB aggregator budget). NFR12 (informed; the new aggregator ClusterRole rules grant `get, list` on `rolebindings`, `clusterrolebindings`, `roles`, `clusterroles`, `networkpolicies`, `deployments`, `replicasets`, `statefulsets`, `daemonsets`, `jobs`, `cronjobs`, `serviceaccounts` per `architecture.md:257` -- no `watch`, no wildcards). NFR15 (satisfied; the `UnavailableReason` field on `WorkloadPosture` carries one of four classes (`transient`, `permission_denied`, `not_found`, `permanent`), never the raw K8s API error text; the full err string is logged via `slog.Error` with `error_class` keyed but never embedded in the LLM-bound payload). NFR35 (satisfied; ≥80% line coverage on `internal/collector/posture/`; integration tests use `sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime/pkg/envtest` (real apiserver + etcd, no kubelet) per `architecture.md:389`). NFR36 (satisfied; integration tests exercise the real typed clientset against a real apiserver -- no mock-only crossings of the K8s-API ring boundary).
- **Architecture reconciliation:** `WorkloadPosture` is defined in `internal/schema/posture.go` (new file, per `architecture.md:728`). The new `internal/keys/workload.go` builders produce the canonical `<namespace>/<owner-kind>/<owner-name>` form (URL-encoded) per `architecture.md:457`, with `<namespace>/Pod/<pod-name>` for orphan pods. The aggregator-vs-controller naming asymmetry is preserved: the codebase uses `aggregator` as the Helm name (`olaitan.aggregator.serviceAccountName`); `architecture.md:257` uses `olaitan-controller` for the same Pod role. The new RBAC rules extend the existing aggregator ClusterRole.
- **First-`client-go` entry cost:** Story 1.11 is the first story to introduce `k8s.io/client-go` and `sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime` to `go.mod`. The actual pins shipped are `k8s.io/api v0.36.0`, `k8s.io/apimachinery v0.36.0`, `k8s.io/apiserver v0.36.0`, `k8s.io/client-go v0.36.0`, and `sigs.k8s.io/controller-runtime v0.24.1`; `k8s.io/cri-api v0.29.10` is retained from Story 1.8 for backward compatibility with the containerd CRI adapter's protobuf surface. These versions deviate from the story spec's pre-implementation pin (v0.29.10 / v0.17.6); the upgrade was applied during implementation when `go mod tidy` resolved the upstream graph and the older pins were no longer reachable. The CI integration-test job needs `setup-envtest` (pinned to `release-0.24` in the Makefile to match controller-runtime) and the `bin/k8s/` apiserver+etcd binaries; the Makefile gains the `envtest-bin` target codifying the one-shot setup. The `ENVTEST_K8S_VERSION` default remains `1.29.x` for the apiserver+etcd image set; bumping to 1.36.x to match the client surface is a follow-up tracked in deferred work.
- **Read-on-demand only:** the package does not use the informer cache (`k8s.io/client-go/informers` or `controller-runtime/pkg/cache`). Every cache miss triggers a fresh K8s API round trip. Continuous watching is explicitly out of scope per `architecture.md:324`; the 60s in-memory TTL covers the freshness budget. Story 3.x's LLM-eligible fresh-fetch path consumes `posture.WithBypassCache()`.
- **Two-API-call ceiling:** the owner-resolver walks at most two K8s API hops per pod (RS → Deployment OR Job → CronJob). Deeper walks return the deepest resolved level rather than continuing.
- **Error redaction:** the `UnavailableReason` class never includes the raw err.Error() text. The four classes (`transient`, `permission_denied`, `not_found`, `permanent`) are the only values the LLM-bound payload sees. Locked in by `TestIntegration_UnavailableReasonStaysClassedNotRaw`.
- **Helm wiring:** the `posture:` values block introduces `enabled` (default true), `cacheTTL` (default 60s; ceiling 60s enforced via `fail` in `templates/configmap.yaml`), and `fetchTimeout` (default 5s). The aggregator ClusterRole gains the read-only RBAC rules per `architecture.md:257`. The configmap bridges all three knobs into the rendered `detection.posture` block via the Story 1.10 P28 per-knob `mustRegexFind + regexReplaceAll` pattern.
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** infrastructure substrate; production evaluation in Epic 5 once the correlator (Story 1.14) consumes the posture client.

### `c3.4-containerd-cri-adapter`

- **Story:** 1.8 (containerd CRI lifecycle adapter).
- **Merge SHA:** `b1a80c4` (PR #17, merged 2026-05-08). Story 1.8 follow-up commit `97728e2` ("Story 1.8 follow-up: address bmad-code-review on PR #17") applied 28 patches (2 decisions resolved + 26 quality patches) on top of the main commit `8da123d` and is covered by this row.
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR3 (satisfied; the adapter ingests containerd CRI `ContainerEventResponse` messages via `RuntimeService.GetContainerEvents` server-streaming RPC and produces canonical `schema.Event` records of `Source=SourceRuntime` + `Category=CategoryLifecycle`). FR6 (satisfied; per-event publish to `subjects.RawRuntime` via `jetstream.WithMsgID(ev.ID)` for at-least-once with server-side dedup over the JetStream 2-minute window). FR8 (partially satisfied; the in-process `sourcehealth.Tracker` is wired and Story 1.12 will bind `Adapter.Health()` to the Prometheus gauge `source_healthy{source="runtime"}`). FR18 (prepared; the adapter emits `attempt:N` tags for sandbox restarts so the Welford engine in Story 1.17 can invalidate per-workload baselines on restart -- the consumer logic is owned by Story 1.17). NFR1 (informed; the bench `BenchmarkAdapter_PublishLatency` measures p50/p99 receive→PubAck on a real embedded NATS server with the AC3 50ms p99 gate baked in via `b.Fatalf` -- production-class measurement under load awaits Story 5.1 eval harness). NFR11 (informed; the host-socket mount is documented in `deploy/helm/olaitan/CRI.md` as privilege-equivalent to the existing Falco socket mount; threat model unchanged from the post-Story-1.6 baseline). NFR35 (satisfied; 85.1% line coverage on `internal/collector/cri/`, measured on the unit-test build only -- integration paths exercised separately under `-tags=integration` and not included in the headline figure -- with the integration test substrate using real boundaries (embedded NATS server + bufconn-backed real `runtime.v1.RuntimeService` gRPC server)). NFR36 (informed; integration test exercises the real protobuf decoder, the real JetStream publish path, and the real CRI gRPC client -- only the runtime-service implementation is in-process via bufconn, mirroring Story 1.6's Falco pattern. Kind-cluster e2e test deferred per Story 1.8 Task 7.6 to a future hardening pass).
- **Architecture / naming reconciliation:** The story epic and ACs read "source containerd / category lifecycle". The schema package (`internal/schema/event.go`) was bootstrapped with `SourceRuntime = "runtime"` (and `CategoryLifecycle = "lifecycle"`) so the adapter axis identifies the architectural layer rather than the specific implementation -- a future CRI-O adapter would publish on the same `RawRuntime` subject. The binding interpretation: `SourceRuntime` + `CategoryLifecycle` satisfies the AC's intent, and downstream correlators read by `Source` rather than by implementation, so the polymorphism is preserved. AC2's "payload includes the workload identity (`namespace/owner-kind/owner-name`)" is satisfied by populating `PodRef{Name, Namespace, UID, Node}` from `pod_sandbox_status.metadata` -- the canonical workload-id resolver (`internal/keys.WorkloadID`, lands in Story 1.11) computes `namespace/owner-kind/owner-name` from `PodRef` plus a K8s API call only at the moment workload-identity is needed, not in the adapter's hot path. Mirrors Stories 1.6 and 1.7.
- **ADRs:** none. The `SourceRuntime` vs `SourceContainerd` naming choice is documented in the package docstring (`internal/collector/cri/translate.go`) and in the PR description rather than as a separate ADR. The host-socket mount security boundary is documented in `deploy/helm/olaitan/CRI.md` rather than as a separate ADR; a future ADR may capture a `containerdSensor.runAsRoot` toggle if/when the chart adds one to lift the nonroot UID issue documented in CRI.md's troubleshooting section.
- **Watchdog design difference vs Stories 1.6/1.7:** CRI lifecycle events are sparse by design (a stable cluster with no Pod churn can go hours without an event). The staleness watchdog therefore only flips `MarkUnhealthy` when staleness AND a non-Ready connection state coincide -- "no events for an hour on a connected stream" is healthy. Locked in by `TestRun_StalenessWatchdog_QuietButConnected_DoesNotFlipUnhealthy`.
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** infrastructure adapter; the bench is for AC3 verification, not for the Epic 5 evaluation harness's per-scenario runs.

### `c3.7.6-incident-finalisation` (Story 4.3)

- **Story:** 4.3 (settling window and incident finalisation).
- **Merge SHA:** `51c02f4` (PR #58, squash-merged to `epic-4-staging` on 2026-06-14 by `olokotoh`). Branch `epic-4/story-4-3-settling-window-and-incident-finalisation` carried the implementation plus two bmad-code-review follow-up commits (round-1 `be17fb9`: per-workload timer generation token + once-only-across-escalation hardening + dropped-CLEAN observability; round-2 `bc5c76c`: lifetime-monotonic generation counter closing the aliasing-across-CLEAN race + msgid disambiguation). Post-merge backfill commit `chore: backfill Story 4.3 merge SHA in traceability matrix` updates this row.
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR42 (satisfied; the settling-window timer fires only when a workload's FSM has been stable in a non-CLEAN state for the configured duration, default 60 s, Helm-tunable), NFR7 (the DFIR-finalisation budget clock starts at settling-window expiry, when `INCIDENTS.finalised` is published), NFR16 (the `INCIDENTS` stream is append-only `LimitsPolicy`/`FileStorage` so the DFIR trigger-of-record cannot be deleted by a consumer ack), NFR35 (>= 80 percent line coverage on `internal/response/settling/`, measured 86.5 percent).
- **Provenance / §3.7.6 reconciliation:** §3.7.6 SUCCEEDS §3.7.5 (`c3.7.5-forensic-capture`, Story 4.2) and is the CONSUMER of 4.2's deferred `INCIDENTS.finalised` hand-off: the 4.2 scope-fence note above explicitly deferred "the settling window / `INCIDENTS.finalised` (Story 4.3)", and the schema-versioning line-36 note deferred the `<incident-id>/` S3 prefix "until Story 4.3 introduces `INCIDENTS.finalised`". This row lands that hand-off: the new `INCIDENTS` JetStream stream (the 12th) and the `incidents.finalised.v1` wire event. Story 4.3 PRODUCES the finalisation event ONLY; the DFIR agent / `ForensicReport` (Story 4.4) is the consumer and is NOT built here (BI-10).
- **Open-assumption decisions:** (1) wired as a `fsm.TransitionSink` MultiSink member (option A), not an `AUDIT.transitions` consumer; (2) FSM history read restart-safe from the Redis `fsm:{workload_id}:history` list via a new `fsm.Store.LoadHistory` reader, with in-process accumulation as the fallback; (3) pinned/overridden non-CLEAN workloads DO finalise; (4) `INCIDENTS` retention 365 d audit-grade by analogy with `AUDIT_*`, Helm-tunable via `response.settling.retention_days` through the existing `StreamConfigsWithRetention` knob; (5) dedup msg id = `package_id + ":" + finalised_at_ns` so a new incident after a CLEAN cycle is not suppressed.
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** `n/a` (no Epic 5 evaluation harness yet; mirrors the c3.7.5 rationale).
- **Architecture omission flagged:** `architecture.md` is not present in the code repo working tree, so the subject-inventory (`architecture.md:225`) and package-tree updates that Story 4.3 Task 8.1 names could not be applied to a file under version control here; this is recorded as a deviation (the BIs already flagged that the architecture omits `INCIDENTS.finalised`). The runbook + schema-versioning + this matrix carry the live contract.

### `c3.7.7-dfir-report` (Story 4.4)

- **Story:** 4.4 (DFIR agent role and ForensicReport schema).
- **Merge SHA:** `62499c4` (squash-merged to `epic-4-staging`, PR #59). Branch `epic-4/story-4-4-dfir-agent-role-and-forensicreport-schema` off `epic-4-staging`.
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR43 (satisfied; the DFIR agent produces an analyst-grade Markdown ForensicReport with YAML front-matter on each `INCIDENTS.finalised`), NFR7 (the `olaitan_report_dfir_duration_seconds` histogram, renamed from `olaitan_dfir_report_generation_seconds` by Story 4.9 §3.7.12, observes the agent-invocation-to-rendered-report latency against the 10s p99 end-to-end target; the durable persist tail is Story 4.6), NFR15 (the pre-LLM `Redact()` fires inside `Analyse` for the DFIR role automatically; the runner interpolates no raw evidence into the prompt or front-matter), NFR35 (>= 80 percent line coverage on `internal/report/dfir/`, measured 82.7 percent unit / 91.8 percent including the integration build).
- **Provenance / §3.7.7 reconciliation:** §3.7.7 SUCCEEDS §3.7.6 (`c3.7.6-incident-finalisation`, Story 4.3) and is the CONSUMER of 4.3's `INCIDENTS.finalised` hand-off: the 4.3 row explicitly deferred "the DFIR agent / `ForensicReport` (Story 4.4) is the consumer and is NOT built here". This row lands that consumer: the durable JetStream consumer (`olaitan-dfir-agent`) on the `INCIDENTS` stream, the `ForensicReport` (`report.v1`) schema triad, the deterministic JSON->Markdown renderer, the `REPORTS.generated` (`reports.generated.v1`) PRODUCER announcement, the `analyst.dfir_provider`/`dfir_model` knobs, the lit-up `RoleDFIR`/`dfir.txt` seam + the `{role="dfir",hash=...}` prompt-version gauge series, and the `AUDIT.assessments` `dfir` row. The agent is downstream of the FSM, never upstream (the trust-bound fence).
- **Open-assumption decisions (PO-ratified + recommended resolutions implemented):** (OA1) durable JetStream consumer on the existing `INCIDENTS` stream (Durable `olaitan-dfir-agent`, `AckExplicitPolicy`, `FilterSubject INCIDENTS.finalised`, bounded `MaxDeliver`), the rules-engine template; (OA2/BI-6) JSON->render hybrid: the LLM returns structured `ForensicReport` JSON validated against the embedded schema, then Go renders YAML-front-matter + Markdown deterministically (PO-ratified); (OA3) embedded schema co-located with the runner under `internal/report/dfir/`, pinned byte-identical to `docs/schemas/forensic_report.json`; (OA4) `REPORTS.generated` carries a backend-neutral `report_url` field populated with the COMPUTED content-addressed key `reports/{yyyy}/{mm}/{dd}/{sha256}.md` (NOT `s3_url`; maps to AC5's "S3 URL"; the durable S3-compatible write + KMS + object-lock are Story 4.6) (PO-ratified); (OA5) degraded-LLM mode is FAIL CLOSED + audit: a provider-unavailable result, a truncated reply, or a post-retry schema failure generates NO report, records the failure in `AUDIT.assessments`, and acks the message (PO-ratified); (OA6) DFIR `MaxTokens` raised to 16000 via per-construction `Config.MaxTokens` (the long-form floor in the safe non-streaming band, well under Opus's 128K streaming-only ceiling) WITHOUT mutating the shared `DefaultMaxTokens`, dev read the claude-api skill before choosing it.
- **Round-1 review follow-up (anti-hallucination + AC6, PO Option A):** the model now returns ONLY the interpretive `narrative` (renamed from `body`); the factual sections (kill-chain timeline, containment actions, MITRE ATT&CK annotations) are assembled DETERMINISTICALLY by the Go renderer from `IncidentFinalised.History` + the chosen `ThreatAssessment` (pod names and raw event ids stripped, control-plane facts only). The narrative is grounded via the contract-safe `Request.PriorAssessment` channel: when no persisted assessment exists the runner synthesises a minimal one from the event whose reasoning is the pod-name-free, event-id-free transition summary. `attack_techniques[]`/posture render an honest "not recorded for this incident" line when unavailable (assessment/posture enrichment deferred to a follow-up "DFIR incident enrichment" story). `callAndValidate` retries a schema violation a bounded number of times (total 2 attempts) before failing closed (AC6). The DFIR prompt is hardened to hard-ground claims and state gaps; new hash `ef3dfff67b5fcdcaef7edb50f73d5e0bb1ab791cdaf1d8f5fdeac5efab8a5157` recorded in `docs/prompt-changelog.md`.
- **Round-2 review follow-up (fix-for-a-fix regressions):** round 2 caught that the round-1 patch left the model-facing schema requiring 8 fields while the prompt asks for narrative-only (a compliant model would fail closed in production, masked by full-object test fixtures), and that `contributing_posture_findings` was still model-supplied. Fix: the schema `required` is now `["narrative"]` (the controller force-stamps every other field), the fakes return narrative-only responses, and posture is force-stamped from the package `WorkloadPosture` (empty in 4.4 -> deterministic "not recorded" section). Final NFR35 coverage 84.8 percent. Merged at `62499c4`.
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** `n/a` (no Epic 5 evaluation harness yet; mirrors the c3.7.5/c3.7.6 rationale).

The runbook + schema-versioning + this matrix carry the live contract.

### `c3.7.8-persistence-redaction` (Story 4.5)

- **Story:** 4.5 (persistence-side redaction extension and full property-test coverage).
- **Merge SHA:** `0f093a0` (squash-merged to `epic-4-staging`, PR #60). Round-1 review fixed the persistence-bound `AUDIT.redactions` `workload_id` (was the package_id); round-2 PASS. Branch `epic-4/story-4-5-persistence-side-redaction-extension-and-full-property-test-coverage` off `epic-4-staging`.
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR44 (satisfied; the redaction policy now applies before persistence as well as before the LLM call), NFR15 (COMPLETED: Story 3.1 shipped the LLM-bound 100 percent, this row lands the persistence-bound 100 percent, proven by the persistence-bound property + adversarial tests), NFR18 (the persistence-bound `AUDIT.redactions` events carry no secret value), NFR35 (>= 80 percent line coverage on the touched packages, measured 89.1 percent `internal/report/redact/` and 86.0 percent `internal/report/dfir/`).
- **Provenance / §3.7.8 reconciliation:** §3.7.8 SUCCEEDS §3.7.7 (`c3.7.7-dfir-report`, Story 4.4) and is the persistence-side REUSE of Story 3.1's `Redact()` (the LLM-bound `c3.7.x-redaction` Epic-3 work): it does NOT add a second pattern engine. The existing UNEXPORTED scrubbers (`keyValueSecretScan`/`jwtScan`/`isSecretKey`) are surfaced through a new EXPORTED `redact.RedactText(s string) (string, []RedactionEvent)` that BOTH the existing `Redact()` (via the shared helpers) AND the new persistence path call (AC1, the ONE pattern engine). The DFIR agent's `Generate` redacts the report's structured regions (narrative/posture/evidence-lists/audit-metadata) through `RedactText` BEFORE `Render` and BEFORE `reportSHA256`/the `REPORTS.generated` announce, so the rendered bytes that the Story 4.6 writer will persist are the REDACTED bytes (4.5 performs NO durable write; the redact package stays a leaf, never importing dfir). This completes NFR15's "100 percent of LLM-bound AND persistence-bound boundaries".
- **PO-ratified decisions (Aslim, 2026-06-14):** (OA1) SHA-OF-REDACTED-BYTES RATIFIED: the `REPORTS.generated` `report_sha256` and the content-addressed `report_url` key are computed over the RENDERED-AND-REDACTED report (what is actually persisted), reinterpreting Story 4.4's announced "SHA of the rendered report" as "SHA of the rendered-and-redacted report"; in the no-secret case the bytes (and SHA) are identical. (OA2) `AUDIT.redactions` wire RATIFIED additive-optional on `audit.redactions.v1`: `source` (closed enum `evidence`/`llm_response`/`posture`/`audit`) + `incident_id` ship as additive OPTIONAL omitempty fields; the Story 3.1 LLM-bound producers stay byte-identical for the empty zero value (the Story 4.1 additive-enum precedent), NO v2 bump. (OA3) `RedactText` exported from `internal/report/redact/` (the ONE-pattern-engine home), not duplicated in dfir. (OA4) per-region `source` derivation: the persistence path redacts the report's structured regions SEPARATELY (narrative -> `llm_response`, posture findings -> `posture`, evidence-derived lists -> `evidence`, audit-metadata fields -> `audit`) so the `source` discriminator is meaningful.
- **Regression gate:** the full Story 3.1 redact suite (`redact_test.go`, `redact_round1_test.go`, `redact_round2_test.go`, `redact_property_test.go`, `redact_integration_test.go`) passes UNCHANGED after the `RedactText` lift (`Redact()` is byte-identical: pure extract-of-shared-helpers, no event-ordering/no-mutation/fail-closed change). The `colonValueSecretScan` text pass is restricted to a single whitespace-free structured key so a prose narrative is not over-redacted (it leaves prose to the `key=value` + JWT scans).
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** `n/a` (no Epic 5 evaluation harness yet; mirrors the c3.7.5/c3.7.6/c3.7.7 rationale).

### `c3.7.9-report-archive` (Story 4.6)

- **Story:** 4.6 (S3 writer with content-addressing, KMS, and object lock).
- **Merge SHA:** `016a80a` (squash-merged to `epic-4-staging`, PR #61). Two-round review: round-1 raised two security candidates (silent non-KMS write, write-failure handling) both verified already-handled in code (no follow-up commit); round-2 PASS. Branch `epic-4/story-4-6-s3-writer-with-content-addressing-kms-and-object-lock` off `epic-4-staging`.
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR45 (satisfied; every redacted `ForensicReport` is written to an S3-compatible object store with content-addressed naming, SSE-KMS, S3 object lock for the Helm-tunable retention period default 90 days, with access-logging + least-privilege IAM documented), NFR7 (the write tail is instrumented on the same synchronous span as the 4.4 generation metric, so the end-to-end generation-to-S3 latency is measurable against the 10s p99), NFR8 (S3 access/secret keys are env secrets, never YAML), NFR35 (>= 80 percent line coverage on the touched packages, measured 93.9 percent `internal/report/archive/` and 86.2 percent `internal/report/dfir/`).
- **Provenance / §3.7.9 reconciliation:** §3.7.9 SUCCEEDS §3.7.8 (`c3.7.8-persistence-redaction`, Story 4.5) and is the durable WRITER consuming the Story 4.5 redacted report + the Story 4.4 content-addressed key. It introduces a NEW `internal/report/archive/` package: a BACKEND-NEUTRAL `ReportArchive` Go interface (`Put` + a `Has` HEAD existence check) and an S3-compatible default implementation (`S3Archive`) reusing the Story 4.2 `minio-go` client + SSE-KMS directive VERBATIM (NO second S3 library; clean-room for future Azure Blob / GCS / filesystem behind the same interface, NOT built). The DFIR agent depends on the INTERFACE, not on `minio-go`; `archive` is a Ring-5 leaf that does NOT import `dfir`. The writer is invoked INLINE in the DFIR `Generate` path AFTER `redactReport` + the SHA, BEFORE the `REPORTS.generated` announce (the redacted bytes only exist in-process there, so a decoupled consumer is impossible): the key PUT is the SAME content-addressed key 4.4 announces (4.6 NEVER recomputes it). The dedup no-op (AC2) is a HEAD-then-skip on the content-addressed key (best-effort against a race; a lost-race "exists/locked" PUT collision is tolerated as a non-fatal dedup outcome, BI-7). Object lock is applied PER OBJECT inline on the PUT directive in the configured mode; the writer uses ONLY `PutObject` + `StatObject` (the least-privilege write-and-head-only set, AC4), never Delete/List/Get/MakeBucket (the object-lock-enabled, versioned bucket is an operator/Helm precondition).
- **PO-ratified decisions (Aslim, 2026-06-14):** (1) BACKEND-NEUTRAL INTERFACE, not AWS-locked: `ReportArchive` interface + S3-compatible default impl reusing the 4.2 `minio-go` client. (2) Content-addressing = the key IS the SHA of the redacted bytes; reuse the 4.4 `contentAddressedKey` + 4.5 SHA, dedup = HEAD-then-skip. (3) IAM least-privilege documented (`docs/iam.md`): writer = `s3:PutObject`/`s3:PutObjectLegalHold`/`s3:HeadObject`; read = separate `olaitan-reporter`. (4) Invocation seam = INLINE in DFIR `Generate` after redaction (synchronous, on the NFR7 path). (5) OA1 object-lock mode = GOVERNANCE default, Helm-tunable to COMPLIANCE; retention Helm-tunable default 90 days. (6) OA4 write-failure = FAIL LOUD + audit + metric, single bounded attempt, NO inline retry / NO deferred queue (Story 4.7 owns that). Open-assumption resolutions: OA2 NEW `report-archive-integration` CI job with an isolated object-lock-enabled bucket (`olaitan-reports-it`); OA3 SEPARATE `ReportArchiveConfig` block (`response.report_archive`), NOT folded under `ForensicsConfig`.
- **Scope fence:** 4.6 does the DIRECT PUT + dedup + object-lock + KMS only. The retry/deferred queue is Story 4.7 (the write metrics `olaitan_report_writes_total{result}` / `olaitan_report_write_duration_seconds` align with 4.7 AC4 so 4.7 extends them; the 4.7 `olaitan_report_writes_deferred_count` gauge is out of scope). The notification webhook is Story 4.8. The full forensic-mode Helm overlay is Story 4.10. 4.6 performs NO redaction (Story 4.5 already did) and NEVER creates the bucket in production code. No native Azure Blob / GCS / filesystem impls (clean-room only).
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** `n/a` (no Epic 5 evaluation harness yet; mirrors the c3.7.5/c3.7.6/c3.7.7/c3.7.8 rationale).
- **Architecture omission flagged:** `architecture.md` is not present in the code repo working tree (the Story 4.3/4.4/4.5 DEVIATION precedent); `docs/runbook.md` (the report-archive write path), `docs/iam.md` (the least-privilege split + bucket preconditions), `docs/schema-versioning.md` (the metric surface) and this matrix carry the live contract.

### `c3.7.10-write-retry-deferred-queue` (Story 4.7)

- **Story:** 4.7 (Forensic write retry and deferred queue).
- **Merge SHA:** `37b3c7e` (squash-merged to `epic-4-staging`, PR #62). Two-round review: round-1 examined the Redis-queue atomicity + deferred-gauge consistency (both verified correct, no follow-up commit); round-2 PASS. Branch `epic-4/story-4-7-forensic-write-retry-and-deferred-queue` off `epic-4-staging`.
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR45 resilience (satisfied; the Story 4.6 fail-loud-no-retry write is REPLACED with retry-then-defer so a transient S3 outage does not lose a forensic record), NFR28 (satisfied; a 60-second S3 outage is tolerated via the short inline retry + the Redis-backed deferred queue + the over-time drain; the deferred depth is observable via the `olaitan_report_writes_deferred_count` gauge, the canonical reconciliation of NFR28's `forensic_writes_deferred_total`), NFR7 (preserved; the inline retry is a fixed sub-NFR7 budget, ~1s worst case, so the synchronous DFIR `Generate` span and the `INCIDENTS.finalised` consumer are NEVER blocked for the 60s window), NFR8 (the deferred-queue Redis password is the `REDIS_PASSWORD` env secret, never YAML), NFR35 (>= 80 percent line coverage on the touched packages, measured 81.1 percent `internal/report/deferq/`, 82.7 percent `internal/report/archive/`, 84.0 percent `internal/redis/`, 86.9 percent `internal/report/dfir/`).
- **Provenance / §3.7.10 reconciliation:** §3.7.10 SUCCEEDS §3.7.9 (`c3.7.9-report-archive`, Story 4.6) and is the RESILIENCE WRAPPER around the Story 4.6 durable writer. It introduces a NEW `internal/report/deferq/` package (the `DeferredQueue` enqueue seam + the `DeferredDrainer.Run(ctx)` background worker + the JSON envelope), a NEW `internal/redis/deferred.go` (typed `reports:deferred` LIST helpers: `EnqueueDeferredReport`/`PeekDeferredReport`/`CommitDeferredReport`/`DeferredReportLen`), and a NEW `archive.IsTransientS3` classifier (beside the 4.6 `isNotFound`/`isAlreadyExistsOrLocked` minio-error helpers). The inline `writeReport` on the DFIR `Generate` span now does a SHORT bounded `retry.Strategy` (the existing `internal/retry` helper, classifying via `IsTransientS3`): a transient failure beyond the short budget DEFERS to the queue (`result="deferred"`) and returns fast; a permanent 4xx misconfiguration keeps the 4.6 fail-loud (`result="error"`, NOT deferred); a brief outage absorbed inline counts `result="retried"`. The drain worker re-PUTs the queued head via the SAME `archive.ReportArchive`, FIFO, committing the `LPOP` only AFTER a confirmed re-PUT (crash-safe), idempotent via the content-addressed key + the 4.6 HEAD-dedup (a double-drain is a deduped no-op). The queue is bounded (`deferred_max_len`, default 1000) and drops the OLDEST on overflow with a loud `olaitan_report_writes_dropped_total{reason="queue_full"}` counter. `archive` stays a Ring-5 leaf (the classifier lives there but it imports no `dfir`/`deferq`); `deferq` depends on the backend-neutral `ReportArchive` interface + the `internal/redis` wrapper, never on `minio-go`/`go-redis` directly, and adds NO score-fold/FSM path (the trust-bound fence).
- **PO-ratified decisions (Aslim, 2026-06-14):** (1) NFR7-vs-60s = SHORT inline retry on the synchronous span then DEFER; the 60s window is the deferred worker's, NOT a 60s inline block (recommend `Min:250ms Max:2s Mult:2 Jitter:1 MaxAttempts:3`). (2) Transient = 5xx/408/429/timeout/connection; permanent = 4xx-misconfig (NOT deferred, dead-lettered on the drain; mirror the claude `isPermanent` + the 4.6 minio `ToErrorResponse` classification). (3) Deferred queue = a Redis LIST at `reports:deferred`, FIFO via RPUSH+LPOP, storing the JSON envelope (bytes + key + PutOptions); reuse the existing `internal/redis` wrapper, no second driver. (4) Drain idempotency via the content-addressed key + the 4.6 HEAD-dedup; commit the LPOP AFTER the successful re-PUT (a double-drain is a deduped no-op). (5) Metrics = EXTEND 4.6's `olaitan_report_writes_total{result}` (+`retried`/`deferred`/`drained`) + `olaitan_report_write_duration_seconds`; ADD the gauge `olaitan_report_writes_deferred_count`; one family, no re-registration. (6) Redis-down-on-enqueue falls back to the 4.6 fail-loud (the irreducible S3-AND-Redis double-outage data-loss window); `Generate` is never blocked or crashed. (7) [OA1] inline retry budget RATIFIED at the recommended values. (8) [OA3] queue-full = bounded (Helm-tunable, default 1000) + DROP-OLDEST + a loud `olaitan_report_writes_dropped_total` counter. Open-assumption resolutions taken: OA2 the gauge-not-counter metric-name reconciliation (the GAUGE `olaitan_report_writes_deferred_count` is the canonical NFR28 metric; the drained-total counter is Story 4.9's); OA5 the drain `Run(ctx)` cadence = `deferred_drain_seconds` default 5s + a wake-on-enqueue channel, in a separate `internal/report/deferq/` package.
- **Scope fence:** 4.7 does the transient/permanent classifier + the short inline retry + the Redis-backed `reports:deferred` queue + the drain worker + the metric reconciliation (extend 4.6's two + add the deferred gauge + the dropped counter) + the simulated-outage integration ONLY. It REUSES the SAME `archive.Put` (it does NOT re-implement the writer, KMS, object-lock, or content-addressing, Story 4.6); it enqueues the ALREADY-redacted bytes (Story 4.5); it touches NO DFIR LLM call / prompt / provider / `REPORTS.generated` schema (Story 4.4). The notification webhook is Story 4.8; the full observability surface (the drained-total counter + the dashboards) is Story 4.9; the forensic-mode Helm overlay is Story 4.10. No second S3 library, no second Redis driver.
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** `n/a` (no Epic 5 evaluation harness yet; mirrors the c3.7.5-c3.7.9 rationale).
- **Architecture omission flagged:** `architecture.md` is not present in the code repo working tree (the Story 4.3/4.4/4.5/4.6 DEVIATION precedent); `docs/runbook.md` (the retry/defer/drain path), `docs/schema-versioning.md` (the metric surface + the deferred-queue envelope) and this matrix carry the live contract.

### `c3.7.11-notification-webhook` (Story 4.8)

- **Story:** 4.8 (Optional incident notification webhook).
- **Merge SHA:** `d60779e` (squash-merged to `epic-4-staging`, PR #63). Round-1 review wired the notify embedded-NATS integration suite into the `report-archive-integration` CI job (the webhook consumer was CI-untested); round-2 PASS. Branch `epic-4/story-4-8-optional-incident-notification-webhook` off `epic-4-staging`.
- **FRs/NFRs:** epics.md Story 4.8 (satisfied; an OPTIONAL, off-by-default outbound HTTP POST webhook fires one operator-facing JSON alert per confirmed-incident report announcement, with configurable-limit exponential-backoff retry and a warn-on-exhaust + delivery metric, honouring the PRD integration table's optionality), NFR8 (the webhook URL is a secret supplied via the `NOTIFICATIONS_WEBHOOK_URL` env/`secretKeyRef`, never YAML, scrubbed to host in logs), NFR18 (the construction log records `webhook_configured`+host, never the token-bearing URL), NFR35 (>= 80 percent line coverage on the touched packages: `internal/report/notify/` measured 84.9 percent with the AC4 httptest + embedded-NATS integration suite included; `internal/config/` and `internal/report/dfir/` stay above 80 percent).
- **Provenance / §3.7.11 reconciliation:** §3.7.11 SUCCEEDS §3.7.10 (`c3.7.10-write-retry-deferred-queue`, Story 4.7) and is the OPTIONAL outbound notification on a confirmed-incident report announcement. THE load-bearing design decision (PO Ratification 1, BI-1/BI-2): the webhook consumes `REPORTS.generated`, NOT `IncidentFinalised` (the report + the REAL written S3 URL exist only on the announce, which fires after the durable write; `IncidentFinalised` lacks both the attack technique and the report URL), and the `ReportGenerated` wire event is EXTENDED ADDITIVELY (omitempty, NO `reports.generated.v1` version bump, the 4.5/4.7 additive-wire discipline) with `threat_score` (from `ForensicReport.ThreatScoreAtDecision`) + `attack_techniques` (from `ForensicReport.AttackTechniques`, which may be empty / "not recorded" per the 4.4 PO Option A deferral) so the AC1 payload is complete; the Story 4.4 PRODUCER is reconciled to populate both fields, and an OLD-shape event without them still decodes (pinned by `TestReportGenerated_OldShapeDecodes`). It introduces a NEW `internal/report/notify/` package: a `Webhook` deliverer (the operator-facing JSON payload build + the configurable-limit `retry.Strategy.Do` POST with the transient/permanent split + the secret-URL scrub) AND a SEPARATE durable `REPORTS.generated` consumer (its OWN cursor `olaitan-notification-webhook`, DISTINCT from the DFIR `olaitan-dfir-agent`, errgroup-launched adjacent to the DFIR agent) so a delivery failure NEVER blocks the synchronous report write or the response-side FSM (BI-3), plus the delivery counter `olaitan_notification_webhook_delivered_total{result}` (BI-9). The payload carries operator-facing metadata ONLY (workload_id, attack_techniques, threat_score, report_url, fsm_state, published_at, idempotency_key = the report SHA256), NO raw evidence (BI-4). On retry exhaust or a permanent 4xx the deliverer logs structured at `warn` (the SCRUBBED host) + counts `result="failed"` and drop-and-acks (never crash-loops the consumer, BI-7). The `notify` package imports only the substrate it operates over + the `dfir.ReportGenerated` type it consumes; it imports NO `internal/decision/score` or FSM package (the trust-bound fence, BI-10).
- **PO-ratified decisions (Aslim, 2026-06-14):** (1) Event source = `REPORTS.generated`, NOT `IncidentFinalised`; `ReportGenerated` extended additively with `threat_score`+`attack_techniques`, no version bump, the 4.4 producer populates them. (2) Off-by-default opt-in: a `NotificationsConfig` block, pointer-tagged `Enabled *bool` default OFF, wired ONLY when enabled AND `webhook_url` non-empty. (3) Separate durable consumer, structurally decoupled: a webhook failure NEVER blocks the write or the FSM; drop-and-ack on undeliverable. (4) Configurable-limit `retry.Strategy` with the transient/permanent split (5xx/429/timeout/connection retry; a 4xx target-rejection is permanent); on exhaust log `warn` + a `result="failed"` metric, never error the consumer. (5) The webhook URL is a SECRET; scrub it in logs (host only). Open-assumption resolutions taken: OA2 the payload key is the honest ARRAY `attack_techniques` (`[]` when not recorded); OA3 the URL is supplied via the `NOTIFICATIONS_WEBHOOK_URL` env-var-overrides-value secret + log-scrub; OA4 accept-duplicate at-least-once with an `idempotency_key` = the report SHA256; OA5 the durable name `olaitan-notification-webhook` + the retry defaults (5 attempts, 1s..30s backoff, multiplier 2, jitter 1, 5s HTTP timeout).
- **Scope fence:** 4.8 builds the OPTIONAL webhook consumer + the additive `ReportGenerated` fields + the producer reconcile + the `NotificationsConfig`/Helm values/configmap-bridge/secret-env + the httptest + embedded-NATS tests + the delivery-counter seam ONLY. It does NOT build: the DFIR agent / `ForensicReport` schema / the `REPORTS.generated` emission (Story 4.4 -- 4.8 CONSUMES + additively EXTENDS it); the persistence redaction (Story 4.5); the durable S3 PUT/KMS/object-lock (Story 4.6); the retry/deferred queue (Story 4.7); the FULL forensic-tier observability surface + dashboards (Story 4.9 -- 4.8 seeds ONLY the webhook delivery counter); the end-to-end Helm forensic-mode chart upgrade (Story 4.10 -- 4.8 adds the `notifications.*` values + configmap bridge only). NO per-vendor (Slack/PagerDuty SDK) code: the one generic JSON POST + the configured URL is the entire surface.
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** `n/a` (no Epic 5 evaluation harness yet; mirrors the c3.7.5-c3.7.10 rationale).
- **Architecture omission flagged:** `architecture.md` is not present in the code repo working tree (the Story 4.3/4.4/4.5/4.6/4.7 DEVIATION precedent); `docs/runbook.md` (the optional-webhook section), `docs/schema-versioning.md` (the additive `ReportGenerated` fields + the delivery counter) and this matrix carry the live contract.

### `c3.7.12-forensic-observability` (Story 4.9)

- **Story:** 4.9 (Forensic-tier observability surface).
- **Merge SHA:** `e2ac03f` (squash-merged to `epic-4-staging`, PR #64). Two-round review (round-1 verified the settling-window gauge is wired at main.go:1391 + zero stale old-metric-name references; round-2 PASS). Branch `epic-4/story-4-9-forensic-tier-observability-surface` off `epic-4-staging`.
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR50 (the report-half forensic metric set is exposed under canonical names: the DFIR generation call counter + duration histogram + settling-window gauge, the S3-writer family, the deferred-drained counter, and the optional webhook delivery counter), NFR32 (every forensic-tier metric is documented with type, unit, label set, and a sample PromQL in the new `docs/metrics.md` reference; the full operator runbook is Epic 6), NFR35 (>= 80 percent line coverage on the touched packages `internal/report/dfir/`, `internal/response/settling/`, `internal/report/deferq/`).
- **Provenance / §3.7.12 reconciliation:** §3.7.12 SUCCEEDS §3.7.11 (`c3.7.11-notification-webhook`, Story 4.8) and is the forensic-tier observability surface (a METRIC-NAME-RECONCILIATION + observability-surface + docs story; it touches NO Claude/Anthropic/Opus code, NO prompt, NO MaxTokens, and changes NO upstream behaviour, the BI-10 scope fence). THE core decision (PO Ratification 1, BI-1/BI-3): the two UNRELEASED Story-4.4 DFIR generation metrics are RENAMED to their AC1 canonical names, safe because Epic 4 is `epic-4-staging`-only (never promoted to `main`, so no dashboard, alert, or external scraper depends on the old names) -- `olaitan_dfir_reports_total{status}` -> `olaitan_report_dfir_calls_total{provider,status}` (the counter gains a `provider` label sourced from the DFIR provider `Name()`, `unknown` when empty per OA1, mirroring the Epic-3 `olaitan_llm_calls_total{provider,role,status}` label precedent) and `olaitan_dfir_report_generation_seconds` -> `olaitan_report_dfir_duration_seconds` (an unlabelled histogram, no label change); the registration AND every test/doc/runbook reference are renamed in one atomic change, the help strings updated to name the canonical metric. A NEW `olaitan_report_settling_window_active{state}` gauge (BI-4/OA2) is added on the Story-4.3 settling controller's `RegisterMetrics` hook, mutated single-threaded on the `Run` goroutine: Inc on the FIRST arm of a window for a workload in a non-CLEAN state (labelled by the candidate `final_state`), Dec on finalisation (`onFire`) or a CLEAN cancel (`onTransition` CLEAN branch); a re-arm of an already-counted workload does NOT double-Inc, and a re-arm that changes the candidate state moves the count from the old state label to the new (a `counted`/`countedState` pair on the `pending` struct tracks the last-counted state for the exact move). A NEW dedicated `olaitan_report_writes_deferred_drained_total` counter (BI-5/OA4, PO Ratification 2) is registered in `NewDFIR` and injected into the drain worker via the existing `deferq.Metrics` seam; it is the SINGLE source of truth for a successfully re-PUT deferred report, and the Story-4.7 `result="drained"` value is RETIRED from the shared `olaitan_report_writes_total{result}` counter (a confirmed drain now increments ONLY the dedicated counter; a HEAD-deduped drain stays `result="deduped"` and a dead-letter stays `result="error"` on the shared counter), so a drain is never double-counted. The S3-writer metrics (`olaitan_report_writes_total{result}`, `olaitan_report_write_duration_seconds`, `olaitan_report_writes_deferred_count`, BI-6) and the webhook delivery counter (`olaitan_notification_webhook_delivered_total{result}`, BI-7) ALREADY carry the canonical AC names + label sets and are CONFIRM-ONLY (registration-assertion tests, no registration change). AC4 (BI-8/PO Ratification 3): 4.9 delivers the canonical metric NAMES/LABELS/TYPES + a NEW `docs/metrics.md` per-metric type/unit/label/PromQL reference (the four NFR32 fields) + the reconcile of the two stale `docs/runbook.md` references to the renamed generation histogram and the Story-4.7 drained paragraph; the FULL operator-runbook prose (alert design, SLO narrative) is Epic 6, exactly as AC4's "(Epic 6)" states.
- **PO-ratified decisions (Aslim, 2026-06-14):** (1) The two DFIR generation metric RENAMES are ratified (epic-4-staging-only, no backward-compat alias). (2) The `olaitan_report_writes_deferred_drained_total` reconcile is ratified: add the dedicated counter AND retire the shared-counter `result="drained"` value (no double-count). (3) The AC4 scope boundary is ratified: canonical metric set + `docs/metrics.md` reference + the two stale-runbook-reference fixes; the full operator-runbook prose is Epic 6. (4) The settling-gauge semantics are ratified: Inc on first arm of a non-CLEAN window, Dec once on finalisation/CLEAN, per-candidate-state depth, re-arm does not double-Inc. Open-assumption resolutions taken: OA1 the empty-provider label value -> the literal `"unknown"`; OA2 the gauge `state` label = the candidate `final_state`, moved on a state-change re-arm; OA3 `docs/metrics.md` as a NEW reference file; OA4 the drained counter is UNLABELLED (a HEAD-deduped drain stays `result="deduped"`, not counted as a drain); OA5 the `result="drained"` value is FULLY retired on the shared counter (no dual-write).
- **Scope fence:** 4.9 delivers the metric set (canonical names/labels/types) + the `docs/metrics.md` reference + the stale-runbook-reference fixes ONLY. It does NOT build: the DFIR agent / `ForensicReport` schema (Story 4.4 -- 4.9 only renames its metrics); the persistence redaction (Story 4.5); the durable S3 PUT/KMS/object-lock (Story 4.6); the retry/deferred queue (Story 4.7 -- 4.9 only adds the drained counter + reconciles the drained value); the webhook controller (Story 4.8 -- 4.9 only confirms its delivery-counter name); the FULL NFR32 operator-runbook prose (Epic 6); the Helm forensic-mode chart upgrade (Story 4.10). It imports NO new package, NO `internal/decision/score` / FSM package, and changes NO upstream behaviour.
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** `n/a` (no Epic 5 evaluation harness yet; mirrors the c3.7.5-c3.7.11 rationale).
- **Architecture omission flagged:** `architecture.md` is not present in the code repo working tree (the Story 4.3-4.8 DEVIATION precedent); `docs/metrics.md` (the canonical forensic-tier metric reference), `docs/runbook.md` (the two reconciled references), `docs/schema-versioning.md` (the 4.9 metric reconciliation row) and this matrix carry the live contract.

### `c3.7.13-helm-forensic-mode` (Story 4.10)

- **Story:** 4.10 (Helm chart wiring for forensic mode -- the TENTH and FINAL Epic-4 story).
- **Merge SHA:** `e2f757f` (squash-merged to `epic-4-staging`, PR #65). Round-1 review fixed a pre-existing Story-4.6 config-bridge defect (the non-parent-scoped `report_archive.retention_days` anchor was clobbering `settling.retention_days` from 365 to 90 on every default render); round-2 PASS. Branch `epic-4/story-4-10-helm-chart-wiring-for-forensic-mode` off `epic-4-staging`.
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR47 (the forensic params -- path / bucket / kms / retention / settling window / webhook -- are wired via Helm values and reflected in rendered manifests), AC1 upgrade-safety (a `helm upgrade` from an Epic-3 RSLT-full deployment installs the Epic-4 surface without disrupting in-flight investigations), AC3 (the three `helm template` goldens regenerated + `helm lint` / `kubeconform -strict` clean), NFR8 (the webhook URL stays a secret env projection, never the ConfigMap). NFR35 (>= 80% coverage) is N/A: NO `internal/` Go is touched; the only Go added is the helm `-tags=helm` table tests + the `//go:build e2e` smoke test, neither a coverage-gated production package.
- **Provenance / §3.7.13 reconciliation:** §3.7.13 SUCCEEDS §3.7.12 (`c3.7.12-forensic-observability`, Story 4.9) and is the Epic-4-CLOSING operator-facing Helm forensic-mode wiring (a HELM-WIRING + values-facade + golden/lint + e2e-test-code + docs story; it touches NO Claude/Anthropic/Opus code, NO prompt, NO MaxTokens, and changes NO controller BEHAVIOUR, the BI-8 scope fence). THE core deliverable (PO Ratification 1, BI-1/BI-3): a thin operator-facing `forensics.*` / `notifications.*` values FACADE (the seven AC2 knobs) that bridges onto the EXISTING `response.{forensics,settling,reportArchive,notifications}` config blocks (Stories 4.2/4.3/4.6/4.7/4.8) + the `secrets.notificationsWebhookUrl` secret. The existing `response.*` blocks are KEPT as the advanced escape hatch (no rename, no removal); the facade OVERLAYS them LAST in `configmap.yaml` (after the per-block bridges + the evaluation-arm overlay), so the facade WINS when both a facade key and its `response.*` counterpart are set. The exact mapping: `forensics.s3.bucket` / `forensics.s3.kms_key_alias` FAN OUT to BOTH the forensic-bundle bucket (`response.forensics.*`) AND the durable-report bucket (`response.report_archive.*`); `forensics.s3.retention_days` targets ONLY the object-lock report bucket (`response.report_archive.retention_days`, parent-scoped so it does NOT touch settling); `forensics.settling_window_seconds` -> `response.settling.window_seconds`; `notifications.enabled` -> `response.notifications.enabled`; `notifications.webhook_url` overlays the `secrets.notificationsWebhookUrl` SECRET projected as `NOTIFICATIONS_WEBHOOK_URL` (facade wins via a `secret.yaml` ternary, NEVER the ConfigMap). The bridges reuse the existing anchored-regex overlay convention (`mustRegexFind` anchor + `regexReplaceAll`, fail-fast on a missing anchor, `$`-doubling, `kindIs "invalid"` nil-checks so a literal 0/false is honoured). BI-2 (CRITICAL, PO Ratification 3): `forensics.path` defaults `fallback` (the only implemented path -- Story 1.4 REJECTED CRIU per ADR-2026-05-02-01: containerd 1.7 lacks `CheckpointContainer`; `ForensicsConfig` has no `Path` field) and the chart `{{ fail }}`s a `forensics.path=criu` selection at template time via the new `olaitan.forensics.validatePath` helper with "CRIU forensic path is not implemented ... Use forensics.path=fallback." (any non-fallback/criu value fails with the valid-values message); `path` bridges to NO config (forward-compat declaration only). BI-5 (AC3): the three goldens (`{default,f,rs}.golden.yaml`) are regenerated (the only delta is the `secret.yaml` facade-comment block -- the rendered config is byte-identical for the default arm because the facade defaults overlay the SAME file-side values, idempotent), `helm lint` is clean, and the new `deploy/helm/helm_test.go` table tests pin each knob's landing config path + the fan-out + the report-only retention + the webhook-is-secret projection + the facade-wins precedence + the criu reject. BI-7 (AC1) the upgrade-safety argument: every Epic-4 controller gate (`response.{forensics,settling,dfir,reportArchive,notifications}.enabled`) defaults `false`, so a default `helm upgrade` renders an EXACT superset of the Epic-3 manifests; NO FSM-persistence-key rename (`fsm:{workload_id}`, Redis, no TTL, restart-recovered) and NO JetStream stream rename (INCIDENTS / REPORTS are new append-only streams); the settling timers re-arm from the rehydrated FSM on a pod restart, so a rolling pod replacement does NOT drop an in-flight settling window. Pinned by `TestForensicsFacade_DefaultsAreAdditiveOffByDefault` + `TestForensicsFacade_RSLTFullArmKeepsEpic4GatesOff` + the README/runbook upgrade-safety paragraphs. BI-6 (AC4, load-bearing, HONEST split, PO Ratification 4): 4.10 delivers the `//go:build e2e`, `OLT_E2E_FORENSICS`-gated `tests/e2e/forensics_smoke_test.go` (S1 escape -> QUARANTINED within NFR7 -> DFIR after settling -> MinIO content-addressed + KMS -> webhook delivers -> the five audit subjects carry payloads) + the MinIO / fake-LLM / notification-sink fixtures + a `make e2e-local-forensics` target; the test COMPILES under `go vet -tags=e2e` but does NOT run in the default CI `e2e` job (RS-arm only; there is NO `e2e-rslt` / `e2e-forensics` CI job yet). The LIVE kind RUN is folded into the carry-forward Epic-3-retro action A1 (the RSLT-full-kind harness, the hard gate before Epic 5); the asserted behaviour is independently covered deterministically by the 4.2-4.8 unit + integration suites (the `forensics-integration` + `report-archive-integration` MinIO CI jobs). This is disclosed honestly in the test header + the runbook + the Makefile (no pretending a live run is wired).
- **PO-ratified decisions (Aslim, 2026-06-14):** (1) the `forensics.*` / `notifications.*` facade KEEPS both surfaces (facade overlays, facade wins). (2) `forensics.s3.bucket` / `kms_key_alias` fan out to BOTH buckets; `retention_days` targets ONLY the report (object-lock) bucket. (3) `forensics.path` defaults `fallback`; `criu` is REJECTED at template time (fail-fast, not a silent no-op). (4) AC4 split: the e2e TEST CODE ships + CI-compiles now; the live kind RUN is gated on the A1 RSLT-full-kind harness before Epic 5. Open-assumption resolutions taken: OA1 keep-both facade+response.* (facade wins); OA2 `notifications.enabled` overlays the existing bool; OA3 the e2e fixtures reuse the Story-3.16 `fake-llm` subcommand + an in-cluster MinIO + an httpbin-style notification sink Deployment; OA4 e2e-code-now / live-run-on-A1; OA5 `notifications.webhook_url` is a secret env projection, never the ConfigMap.
- **Scope fence:** 4.10 is Helm wiring + the values facade + the goldens regen + `helm lint`/`kubeconform` + the helm table tests + the `//go:build e2e` forensics smoke-test CODE + fixtures + docs ONLY. It builds NO new controller (Stories 4.2-4.9 own those), changes NO controller behaviour, builds NO CRIU capture path (BI-2 rejects `criu`), adds NO Grafana dashboard / alert rule / Epic-6 runbook narrative, and writes NO Epic-5 evaluation harness (`cmd/olaitan-eval/`, the four-way matrix, the statistical pipeline -- the NEXT epic). NO `internal/` Go is changed.
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** `n/a` (no Epic 5 evaluation harness yet; mirrors the c3.7.5-c3.7.12 rationale).
- **Architecture omission flagged:** `architecture.md` is not present in the code repo working tree (the Story 4.3-4.9 DEVIATION precedent); `deploy/helm/README.md` (the facade recipe + the upgrade-safety paragraph), `docs/runbook.md` (the Helm forensic-mode wiring + upgrade-safety + criu-rejected note), `docs/schema-versioning.md` (the 4.10 "no wire change" row) and this matrix carry the live contract.

### `c3.7.x-operator-override-pin` / `c3.7.x-operator-override-ttl`

- **Story:** 2.7 (operator override annotations and TTL, FR38/FR39).
- **Merge SHA:** `014266a` (PR [#35](https://github.com/olokotoh/olaitan/pull/35), squash-merged to the `epic-2-staging` integration branch 2026-06-04; pending review before `main`). Closed under THREE bmad-code-review rounds plus Copilot: round 1 fixed a spurious-release-on-transient-error bug and replaced the TTL refreshing-lease with an edge-triggered hard deadline (honouring FR39); round 2 fixed regressions in that redesign (sub-poll-TTL re-pin loop, owner-scaled-to-zero false release) with authoritative FSM-pin-based expiry and owner-confirmed manual removal; round 3 verified clean (READY TO MERGE).
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR38 (satisfied; an `olaitan.io/state-override` annotation pins the FSM and defers ThreatScore transitions via the in-fsm `Pin`/`ReleasePin` seam consulted by `Evaluate`). FR39 (satisfied; the `override:{workload_id}` Redis key carries a native TTL equal to the requested duration and the poll/reconcile releases the pin on native-TTL expiry or manual annotation removal, then resumes score-driven control). NFR35 (touched-package coverage held: override 82.5%, fsm 84.5%). NFR8 (the controller's dedicated Redis client requires `REDIS_PASSWORD` AUTH).
- **ADRs:** none (the two load-bearing decisions, poll-over-watch observation (BI-1) and the in-fsm `Pin`/`ReleasePin` seam (BI-2), are recorded as Binding Interpretations in the story artefact rather than as separate ADRs).
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** response-ring control-plane behaviour, not an Epic-5 evaluation-bearing claim.
- **Dev Agent Record (round-2 bmad-code-review follow-up).** Five regressions in the round-1 hard-deadline/edge-triggered redesign were fixed on `epic-2/story-2-7-operator-override-annotations-and-ttl`:
  - **FIX 1 (HIGH) — authoritative absolute-deadline expiry** (`internal/response/override/controller.go`, `detectHardDeadlineExpiry`). The fragile two-tick `lastActive` level-edge detector (a key had to be seen present on the previous tick then absent this tick) was REPLACED: a workload is hard-deadline-expired when it is CURRENTLY PINNED in the FSM (`IsPinned`, the authority for "we believe this is pinned"), still desired this tick (in `desiredSet`), has NO active Redis key this tick, and is not indeterminate. The missing key IS the deadline signal. The `lastActive` field and its per-tick bookkeeping were DELETED. This fixes (a) `ttl < pollInterval` (key written and expired within one poll gap, never captured by `lastActive` => the old code's infinite re-pin loop) and (b) the SCAN/GET race in `ListActive` dropping the key on the would-be "last active" tick (=> pin stranded). The expiry phase runs BEFORE the apply phase so the `consumed` marker suppresses a same-tick re-pin. New test `TestReconcile_TTLShorterThanPollExpiresOnce` (ttl 1s, annotation kept present) asserts exactly ONE pin then a release with NO per-tick re-pin loop; the existing `TestReconcile_HardDeadlineReleasesWithAnnotationPresent` (ttl>=poll) still passes.
  - **FIX 2 (MED) — per-workload release suppression + owner-confirmed manual removal** (`internal/response/override/controller.go` `detectManualRemovals`, `computeDesired`; `internal/response/override/identity.go` `confirmOwnerAnnotation`). (a) The tick-wide `resolveIncomplete` veto that skipped ALL manual removals on any single pod's resolve error was REMOVED; protection is now PER WORKLOAD via the `indeterminate` set plus a pod-sourced-pin guard keyed on `resolveFailed`. (b) Before releasing a candidate as a manual removal, the annotation's absence is CONFIRMED by a direct GET of the owner object (parse `workload_id` -> typed-client GET -> check for `olaitan.io/state-override`): owner NotFound or owner-without-annotation => release; owner WITH annotation => not a removal (pods merely absent, e.g. scaled to zero) => retain; owner GET error => indeterminate => retain. This closes the owner-scaled-to-zero / rollout-churn false positive. New test `TestReconcile_OwnerScaledToZeroNotReleased` (zero pods but owner still annotated => retained; owner annotation removed => released).
  - **FIX 3 (MED) — surface the parseOverride TTL parse error** (`internal/response/override/store.go` `parseOverride`). `ttl_seconds` (and `applied_at_ns`) parse errors are now surfaced rather than coerced to 0; `ListActive` already skips a key on a parse error, so a malformed key reads as "no active key" => a clean single re-apply (self-healing), not the per-tick re-pin + TTL-refresh churn a ttl=0 signature mismatch caused. New test `TestStore_ListActiveSkipsMalformedTTL`.
  - **FIX 4 (LOW) — consumed/last-rejected prune backstop** (`internal/response/override/controller.go` `pruneInMemory`). On each successful tick, entries for workloads that are neither desired, pinned, nor active are pruned, bounding the in-memory maps during a sustained outage; a currently-rejected workload's marker is exempt so FIX 5's dedup holds.
  - **FIX 5 (LOW) — rejected counter vs event consistency** (`internal/response/override/controller.go` `emitRejection`, `lastRejected` map). A standing invalid annotation now increments `olaitan_response_override_rejected_total` and emits its NATS event ONCE (tracked by a per-workload `reason|requested` signature), consistent with the deduped event; a changed-but-still-invalid value re-counts once. Documented in `docs/runbook.md`. New test `TestReconcile_StandingRejectionCountsOnce`.
  - **Gate status:** `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go vet -tags=integration ./internal/response/override/...`, full `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./internal/response/override/... ./internal/response/fsm/...` all green; `golangci-lint run ./...` => 0 issues. Round 2 is code-and-docs only; no new commit (left for the merge gate).

### `c3.7.x-audit-subjects`

Closed under two bmad-code-review rounds plus a Copilot check (no Copilot comments posted): round 1 (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor) fixed a MED schema-violation bug — `auditReconcileDelete` could emit a schema-invalid `fsm_state=""` for a managed policy whose `AnnFSMState` annotation was stripped/corrupt; guarded by `isAuditableFSMState` (skip emit, still count) and added the real-`fsm.Machine` transitions test proving BI-1.1; round 2 (regression hunter + completeness) confirmed the fix sound (race-clean x3, deterministic) and applied one low-severity item — the collector ring now provisions the AUDIT_* streams with the same `StreamConfigsWithAudit(retention)` as the aggregator. Final CI green across go + helm + docker + e2e + traceability.
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR40 (satisfied; three append-only NATS subjects `AUDIT.transitions`/`AUDIT.overrides`/`AUDIT.policies` capture every FSM transition, override application/rejection, and NetworkPolicy mutation with full attribution). NFR16 (satisfied; each rides a dedicated `LimitsPolicy` JetStream stream — append-only by retention, consumers cannot delete events — guarded by `TestAuditStreamsAreLimitsPolicy`). NFR35 (touched-package coverage held: audit 83.8%/82.3%, netpol 85.1%, config 83.2%, nats 85.9%; override's envtest-gated suite keeps it at the shipped ~82.5% in CI). NFR40 (committed draft-2020-12 JSON-Schema in `docs/schemas/audit/*.json` with `*.yaml` mirrors).
- **ADRs:** none (the twelve binding interpretations BI-1..BI-12 are recorded in the story artefact rather than as separate ADRs; the architecture-vs-AC 90-day transitions retention reconciliation is documented in `docs/schema-versioning.md`).
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** response-ring SIEM audit substrate, not an Epic-5 evaluation-bearing claim.
- **Bundled CI fix.** A latent e2e `InvalidImageName` failure (surfaced while clearing the staging-tip red, operator-directed) was root-caused and fixed: `helm --set image.tag=<all-numeric-short-sha>` type-infers an int64, so the chart's `printf "%s:%s"` rendered `olaitan:%!s(int64=...)`. Fixed with `toString` in the `olaitan.image` helper (`deploy/helm/olaitan/templates/_helpers.tpl`) plus `--set-string` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and the `e2e-local` Makefile target. Deterministic for the ~3% of commits whose short SHA is all-digits, which is why the all-numeric staging-tip commit failed while its hex-containing parent passed.

### `c3.6.3-redaction-*` (Story 3.1)

- **Story:** 3.1 (Pre-LLM and pre-persistence redaction pipeline; FR44/NFR15/FR41-redactions/NFR18). Epic 3 opening story: the reusable `Redact()` over `EvidencePackage` plus the fifth and final append-only SIEM subject `AUDIT.redactions`.

Two bmad-code-review rounds plus a pre-merge leak-verification. Round 1 (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Acceptance Auditor) caught three BLOCKER secret-leak paths (the Kubernetes name/value env-var shape, a JWT embedded inside a larger `Event.Raw` string, and an `Event.Tags` bypass). Round 2 caught two under-redaction regressions that the round-1 over-redaction fixes introduced (dotted/slashed secret keys, and base64 or printable-ASCII secrets under `data`/`payload`/`bytes` keys, the canonical Kubernetes Secret shape) plus a Tags key=value gap. The pre-merge verification confirmed no third-order regression and that benign evidence survives. Copilot review is not configured on this repo.
