### `c3.6.x-audit-assessments` (Story 3.14)

- **Story:** 3.14 (AUDIT.assessments append-only audit subject; FR41, NFR15/NFR18/NFR29/NFR16). Expands the Story 3.8 MINIMAL `AuditAssessment` (v1) to the full v2 SIEM payload: per-role prompt-version hashes (Story 3.13), providers/models, the L1Hypothesis/L2Verification/ThreatAssessment verdicts, the REDACTED evidence inputs, raw + capped confidences, and `redaction_applied`. The cmd caller redacts the package at the audit boundary via the same pure `redact.Redact` the providers ran, so the recorded inputs are byte-identical to what the LLM saw and carry no secret values (NFR15/NFR18). Schema at `docs/schemas/audit/assessments.{json,yaml}` (draft 2020-12, additionalProperties:false), validated against three committed exemplars (full/l1_l2/l1_only) + a built event + a drift-rejection set; an adversarial secret+JWT package proves no secret reaches the payload. `AUDIT.assessments` retention stays 365 d, now Helm-tunable via `response.audit.retention_assessments_days`.
- **BI-2a (ring-clean seam):** `internal/response/audit` (ring layer 4) does NOT import `internal/decision/analyst` (layer 3, NFR38). `AssessmentFromChain` takes a new `AssessmentInput` of `schema` types; `cmd/olaitan` (the only ring importing both) projects the `analyst.ChainResult` onto it.
- **BI-6 fence:** the operational `ASSESSMENTS.completed` 30-day read-model named in the architecture is deferred (no Epic-3 consumer reads it; introducing a stream with no publisher/consumer would be dead infrastructure). The SIEM copy on `AUDIT.assessments` (365 d) is the load-bearing trail this story owns.
- **Merge SHA:** `5f183de` (PR [#53](https://github.com/olokotoh/olaitan/pull/53), squash-merged to `epic-3-staging` 2026-06-13). Two bmad-code-review rounds (Copilot not configured on this repo). Round 1 (Blind+Edge+Acceptance): Acceptance NO VIOLATIONS, ring-clean confirmed; 4 patches in `1f680c8` (per-role metadata gated on AgentsAvailable + empty-omission; retention validation; AC3 static guard; tighter assertions/doc). Round 2 (Regression+Completeness): Completeness PASS; found the round-1 gating fix NOT-PROVEN by its shipped test, added two MUTATION-PROVEN ChainResult-driven regression tests in `7bac161`. CI green (go, helm, docker, e2e, traceability, prompt-changelog).
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR41 (assessments subject, satisfied); NFR15/NFR18 (redaction at the audit boundary, property + adversarial tests); NFR29 (v1->v2 MINOR additive); NFR16 (LimitsPolicy append-only). FENCED: olaitan_llm_prompt_version + per-role-latency metrics (3.15); Helm eval presets (3.16); DFIR report (Epic 4).
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** audit-plumbing claims proven by unit + schema + adversarial tests; no evaluation numbers attach.

### `c3.6.3-provider-ollama-*` (Story 3.4)

- **Story:** 3.4 (Ollama provider implementation for air-gapped deployments; FR48, NFR18, NFR35, NFR36, NFR38). The third and final concrete implementation of the Story 3.2 `Provider` interface, plus the first Helm surface of Epic 3 (optional in-cluster Ollama) and the first non-"api" cmd/olaitan wiring branch.
- **Merge SHA:** `26aeb82` (PR [#43](https://github.com/olokotoh/olaitan/pull/43), squash-merged to the `epic-3-staging` integration branch 2026-06-12; pending epic-end promotion to `main`). Two bmad-code-review rounds (Copilot review is not configured on this repo). Round 1 (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor) triaged 0 decision-needed / 10 patch / 1 defer / 7 dismissed; the two HIGHs were invisible to the story's own tests: the score-cap chain was broken end-to-end (file-side `score_cap: 35` with no bridge or overlay key, so every deployment incl. the air-gapped reference ran the Ollama tier at Claude-level trust while the docs claimed 25) and the release NetworkPolicy UNIONED onto the ollama pod via the shared name/instance labels, re-granting the ingress/egress the ollama policy denies. All ten applied in `d80f11e`. Round 2 (Regression Hunter + Completeness Verifier) returned Completeness PASS with every round-1 patch MUTATION-PROVEN (fix reverted, test failed) and three findings in the fixes themselves, fixed in `c420622`: the score_cap bridge's `with` gate swallowed a literal 0 (zero trust silently became 35), the bare-`#` endpoint form slipped past validation (url.Parse has no fragment analog of ForceQuery), and kubeconform never validated the gated render. CI green (go, helm, docker, e2e, traceability) on every SHA.
- **Transport decision (BI-2):** the NATIVE Ollama `/api/chat` endpoint per architecture.md:381, NOT Ollama's OpenAI-compat shim (the native shape carries done_reason and the eval counts; the dedicated package is the architecture file-tree mandate). Hand-rolled stdlib client, no new dependency. `stream: false` is EXPLICIT and load-bearing (the server defaults to streaming; an NDJSON leak fails the single-object decode and degrades transient -- pinned by test). `options.num_predict` is the output ceiling (the native name); no sampling params, no `num_ctx`, no `keep_alive` on the wire.
- **No-credential divergence (BI-3):** architecture.md:263 -- the in-cluster NetworkPolicy is the auth boundary. `New` takes no apiKey, no Authorization header exists (its ABSENCE is asserted on the wire), and the NFR18 construction log carries no `api_key_set` field. `Model` is constructor-REQUIRED (`ErrNoModel`); `Endpoint` defaults to `http://ollama.olaitan.svc.cluster.local:11434` and is validated fail-fast (`ErrBadEndpoint`, never echoed).
- **Honesty bindings:** `ScoreCap` defaults to 25 (the PRD per-provider trust ladder 35/30/25; Story 3.7 owns enforcement). `MaxContextTokens` defaults to 4096 -- the server-side `num_ctx` scale, NOT the model's theoretical window, because Ollama clamps prompts to `num_ctx` regardless of model capability and this provider never sends it; advertising 128K to the 3.5-3.7 prompt budgeter while the server truncates would be the config-lies-about-capability failure mode. Health is one real 1-token chat call: a cold model load exceeds the 10s bound by design (unhealthy-until-warm is signal).
- **Wiring (BI-6):** under the EXISTING config value `local` (`internal/config` untouched; `analyst.local.{endpoint,model}` is the schema home). The PRD-level spelling `analyst.provider: ollama` arrives as a Helm alias with Story 3.16. Case-insensitive gate; empty model degrades to rules-only (claude empty-key parity); `_ = ollamaProvider` held for the 3.5-3.7 orchestrator.
- **Helm (BI-7):** `ollama.enabled` default FALSE -- the default render is byte-identical to the pre-3.4 chart (asserted by diff during dev and by the gate test). Enabled: Deployment + ClusterIP Service + NetworkPolicy (ingress ONLY from the aggregator pod selector on 11434; egress declared and EMPTY -- the air-gap statement; the AC's "controller ServiceAccount" reconciles to the aggregator pod selector because NetworkPolicy selects pods). `values-airgapped.yaml` is the FR48 reference overlay (architecture.md:963), made real by two new C11-pattern value->config bridges for `analyst.local.{endpoint,model}` (previously only `analyst.provider` was bridged; an overlay setting them would have been a silent no-op).
- **Metric family:** shared `olaitan_llm_calls_total` grows to 3 providers x 4 roles x 4 statuses = 48 bounded series; the tri-provider same-registry test drives a REAL increment per provider (the Story 3.3 round-1 lesson encoded).
- **Documented amendments (round-1 review, 2026-06-12):** (a) BI-8's "no release-networkpolicy.yaml edit" fence is AMENDED: the ollama pod carries the shared name/instance labels and NetworkPolicies union, so without an ollama-component exclusion on the release policy's podSelector the release policy's ingress (every olaitan pod, any port) and egress (DNS/apiserver/intra-namespace/extraEgress) re-granted exactly what the ollama policy denies; the exclusion is gated on `ollama.enabled` so the default render stays byte-identical. (b) BI-7.5 extended: a THIRD bridge for `analyst.score_cap` (the file-side default is the Claude-tier 35; without the bridge every deployment incl. the air-gapped reference silently ran the Ollama tier at 35 while the docs claimed 25 -- the exact trap class BI-7.5 names), `$`-doubling + `toString` on all bridged values (regex capture-group injection / rune-literal corruption), and a DERIVED endpoint (rendered Service DNS for the actual release/namespace) when `ollama.enabled` with no operator endpoint (the previous hardcoded overlay endpoint dialed a nonexistent Service under any other install name). (c) The ollama Deployment gains probes, `automountServiceAccountToken: false`, `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`, a required image tag, and `extraEnv`/`nodeSelector`/`tolerations` passthroughs (the runbook's num_ctx advice was unimplementable without an env hook). (d) `done:false` in a 200 single object now degrades transient (severed-stream partial, never a truncated success) and endpoint validation also rejects query/fragment forms. (e) The wiring warns loudly when `analyst.score_cap` exceeds the Ollama-tier 25 on the local path (observability, not enforcement; 3.7 owns the cap).
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR48 (air-gapped mode: package + overlay + NetworkPolicy isolation incl. the union exclusion). NFR18 (no-credential divergence documented; construction-log field assertions; redaction-before-send captured-body proof). NFR35 (coverage: ollama 91.3% at the round-1 follow-up). NFR36 (httptest real `/api/chat` boundary; attempt counts by request counting). NFR38 (recursive ring guard covers the package for free).
- **ADRs:** none (BI-1..BI-8 recorded in the story artefact). **eval_run_ids rationale:** transport substrate; the air-gapped evaluation cell is Epic 5 scope.

### `c3.6.3-provider-openai-*` + `c3.6.3-provider-shared-*` (Story 3.3)

- **Story:** 3.3 (OpenAI-compatible provider implementation; FR25 heterogeneous-model orchestration substrate, NFR18, NFR35, NFR36, NFR38). The second concrete implementation of the Story 3.2 `Provider` interface, dropped in WITHOUT interface change.
- **Merge SHA:** `97f2276` (PR [#42](https://github.com/olokotoh/olaitan/pull/42), squash-merged to the `epic-3-staging` integration branch 2026-06-10; pending epic-end promotion to `main`). Two bmad-code-review rounds (Copilot review is not configured on this repo). Round 1 (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor) triaged 0 decision-needed / 8 patch / 1 defer / 10 dismissed; the two HIGHs: the BI-7 dual-provider coexistence test registered but never incremented the claude series, and upstream error-body snippets could carry an echoed API key into error strings and logs (OpenAI echoes key material in 401 bodies). All eight applied in `22f6448` with biting regression tests. Round 2 (Regression Hunter + Completeness Verifier) returned Completeness PASS and two LOWs in the round-1 fixes themselves, fixed in `a949978`: a rune-boundary-vacuous sanitize test and a partial-key-prefix window on mid-read failures (snippet now dropped on read error). CI green (go, helm, docker, e2e, traceability) at merge; the one e2e failure during review was a gcr.io 500 pulling the distroless base image, cleared on re-run.
- **Transport decision (BI-2):** a HAND-ROLLED stdlib net/http client speaking the lowest-common-denominator Chat Completions shape (`POST {base}/chat/completions`, `max_tokens` not `max_completion_tokens`, `Authorization: Bearer`, no sampling params, `stream:false`), per architecture.md:309's "generic OpenAI-compatible HTTPS client". The official openai-go SDK is deliberately NOT added: compatible servers (Together, Groq, LiteLLM, vLLM) implement a subset of the OpenAI surface and the common denominator is the contract. No new dependency.
- **Shared-metric refactor (BI-3):** `olaitan_llm_calls_total` registration hoisted to `provider.RegisterCallsMetric` with `prometheus.AlreadyRegisteredError` reuse so multiple providers coexist in one process (the Story 3.8 routing prerequisite); `claude.New` amended to use it (behaviour-identical solo). Family ceiling now 2 providers x 4 roles x 4 statuses = 32 bounded series. Shared transport helpers (status enum, role-timeout table, final-status resolution, analyst user-turn composition) hoisted to `internal/agent/provider/transport.go`; the Claude provider consumes them via aliases, its Story 3.2 test suite green unchanged.
- **Identity:** `Name() == "openai"` is the family identity for the metric label and the 3.8 routing key; the ENDPOINT distinguishes the vendor. `Model` is constructor-REQUIRED (`ErrNoModel`) -- the one documented divergence from claude.New's defaulting, because no cross-vendor default model exists. `MaxContextTokens` is a config knob (default 128000), not a model map.
- **Scope fence (BI-6):** NO cmd/olaitan wiring (no claude-vs-openai discriminator exists under `analyst.provider: api`; Story 3.8 owns routing), no config/schema/retry/metrics/redact change.
- **Documented amendments (round-1 review, 2026-06-10):** (a) the construction log carries a `base_url` field beyond the Story 3.2 BI-4 field set (the endpoint is the one knob distinguishing compat vendors); safe because `New` now validates BaseURL (absolute http(s), no userinfo, `ErrBadBaseURL` fail-fast). (b) The `olaitan_llm_calls_total` help string moved into `provider.RegisterCallsMetric` with its wording updated ("bounded 16-series" dropped: the family ceiling is 32 post-3.3); safe because the helper is the family's sole registrant. Round-1 hardening beyond the spec: upstream error-body snippets are laundered before reaching error strings/logs (key scrub, control-char flattening, rune-safe cut; OpenAI echoes key material in 401 bodies), redirects are never followed (`CheckRedirect`: a 3xx surfaces as a typed transient `apiError` instead of converting the POST to a GET and re-sending the bearer token), and non-200 bodies are read to a 4 KiB bound instead of the 10 MiB success-path ceiling.
- **FRs/NFRs:** NFR18 (Bearer-key hygiene sentinel tests incl. upstream key-echo scrub; redaction-before-send captured-body proof). NFR35 (coverage: openai_compat 91.3%, provider 91.9%, claude 94.3%; measured at the round-1 follow-up). NFR36 (httptest real-wire Chat Completions boundary; attempt counts by request counting). NFR38 (the recursive ring guard covers the new package for free). Story 3.2 round-1 lessons applied by construction: 200-null-body/empty-choices guards (retryable, never success, never panic), no case-sensitive gates, key treated as opaque.
- **ADRs:** none (BI-1..BI-7 recorded in the story artefact). **eval_run_ids rationale:** transport substrate; the empirical LLM evaluation is Epic 5 scope.

### `c3.6.3-provider-*` (Story 3.2)

- **Story:** 3.2 (LLM provider abstraction and Claude implementation; FR19-FR29 transport substrate, NFR4/NFR5, NFR18, NFR23 observability, NFR27 clean degradation, NFR36, NFR38). The transport tier every LLM-facing path (Stories 3.5-3.7 L1/L2/Senior and Epic 4's DFIR agent) goes through.
- **Merge SHA:** `ddc9cfb` (PR [#41](https://github.com/olokotoh/olaitan/pull/41), squash-merged to the `epic-3-staging` integration branch 2026-06-09; pending review before `main`). Two bmad-code-review rounds (Copilot review is not configured on this repo). Round 1 (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor) caught two HIGHs and one MED, all empirically reproduced: a 200 response with a JSON `null` body decodes to a nil `*Message` with no SDK error, panicking `toResponse` while the deferred metric recorded `success` (and `Health` reported healthy); the `cmd/olaitan` provider switch was case-sensitive while config validation lowercases, so `analyst.provider: "API"` validated but silently disabled the whole LLM tier (plus a dead `"claude"` arm); and the projected Secret value was untrimmed, so a kubectl-style trailing newline failed every call client-side as `transient_failure`. All fixed in the follow-up (`9d2e0ae`) with regression tests proven to bite by semantic revert. Round 2 (regression hunter + completeness verifier) confirmed the fixes correct and complete, race-clean x2, every round-1 LOW documented (score_cap-0 coercion, Prompt/PriorAssessment redaction contract, empty-Raw failed-verdict rule), and recorded two non-blocking LOWs: a pathological-Unicode validator/wiring edge (U+0130 in `analyst.provider`) and pre-existing go.mod tidy drift dating from Story 3.1 test imports. CI green (go, helm, docker, e2e, traceability) on both review SHAs. Final coverage: provider 100%, claude 93.7%.
- **Claim-id reconciliation:** the story spec proposed the `c3.2-provider-*` family, but the matrix's claim_id grammar binds `c<chapter>.<section>` to a real `report/chapter-3-methodology.md` heading and §3.2 is "Research Philosophy and Design Approach". The provider transport belongs to §3.6.3 "Tier 3: LLM Analyst" (the same anchor as the Story 3.1 redaction rows), so the family is `c3.6.3-provider-*`. Documented here and in the PR body; not a silent deviation.
- **Layout reconciliation (BI-1):** the `Provider` interface and the Claude implementation live at `internal/agent/provider/` and `internal/agent/provider/claude/` per architecture.md:292/:308/:927. The empty `internal/decision/analyst/` directory (only `.gitkeep`) is a placeholder for the Story 3.5-3.7 agent code, NOT the provider home; it is untouched.
- **Deferral resolution (architecture.md:308):** official `github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-go` chosen over a raw HTTPS client (stable, typed error classes, custom base URL for the test boundary, structured-output support). The SDK's auto-retry is disabled (`option.WithMaxRetries(0)`); `internal/retry` is the single observable source of truth (BI-2).
- **FRs/NFRs:** NFR4/NFR5 (per-role total-budget timeouts 30/30/60/120s). NFR18 (API key never logged/metricised/error-embedded -- sentinel-key tests; evidence redacted via Story 3.1's `RedactAndAudit` BEFORE the wire payload is built -- captured-request-body test). NFR23 (transport bounded + observable via `olaitan_llm_calls_total{provider,role,status}`, 16 bounded series; the circuit breaker itself is Story 3.12). NFR27 (no key => provider not constructed, controller runs rules-only; the fallback policy is Story 3.10). NFR35 (coverage: provider 100%, claude 93.5%). NFR36 (httptest + `option.WithBaseURL` real-wire-format boundary suite; attempt counts proven by request counting, not wall-clock). NFR38 (ring guard test: no decision/response/correlator/collector/dfir import; no redact back-edge).
- **ADRs:** none (the nine binding interpretations BI-1..BI-9 are recorded in the story artefact).
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** LLM transport substrate; the empirical LLM evaluation is Epic 5 scope, not a 3.2 claim.

### `c3.6.7-fsm-preserved-killed` (Story 4.1)

- **Story:** 4.1 (PRESERVED+KILLED FSM state extension; FR31). Adds the fifth FSM state `PRESERVED_KILLED`, reachable ONLY from QUARANTINED via a Helm-tunable kill condition (default ThreatScore >= 90 sustained 300 s after the QUARANTINED apply) OR an operator override from QUARANTINED. State transition + audit only; it does NOT delete the pod, run CRIU/forensic capture, or invoke the DFIR agent (those are Stories 4.2/4.3/4.4, BI-13).
- **Merge SHA:** `1366fce` (PR #56, squash-merged to `epic-4-staging` on 2026-06-14). Branch `epic-4/story-4-1-preserved-killed-fsm-state-extension` off `epic-4-staging`.
- **ch3_section reconciliation:** the FSM family runs `c3.6.4-fsm-escalation` / `c3.6.5-fsm-deescalation` / `c3.6.6-fsm-state-persistence` (§3.6.4/§3.6.5/§3.6.6). The next free slug is therefore `c3.6.7-fsm-preserved-killed` at `§3.6.7` (the create-story draft proposed `c3.6.6`, which collided with the shipped Story 2.3 `c3.6.6-fsm-state-persistence`; corrected to `c3.6.7` at backfill). The methodology chapter (`report/chapter-3-methodology.md`) lives in the thesis repository, not the code repository, so the heading is asserted by slug continuity with the shipped §3.6.4/§3.6.5/§3.6.6 FSM rows. If §3.6.7 is not yet a real heading in the thesis, this row attaches under the nearest §3.6 FSM heading per the §3.6.3 precedent.
- **BI reconciliation (BI-2):** Story 2.2 BI-7 (`PRESERVED_KILLED unreachable`) and Story 2.7's override-state rejection of `PRESERVED_KILLED` are INTENTIONALLY superseded by this story: PRESERVED_KILLED becomes reachable, but ONLY via the single legal QUARANTINED -> PRESERVED_KILLED edge. The `TestProperty_PreservedKilledUnreachable` property is reframed to `TestProperty_PreservedKilledOnlyFromQuarantined`; the `c3.6.4`/`c3.6.5` rows that cited the old name are updated. `schema.ValidTransition` / `schema.stateOrderMap` are UNCHANGED (the single-legal-entry structural invariant was already enforced there).
- **FRs/NFRs:** FR31 (the final arrow of the `CLEAN -> ... -> QUARANTINED -> PRESERVED+KILLED` chain). FR47/FR49 (the kill tunables are hot-reloadable thresholds, read per-Evaluate). FR55/BI-12 (the benign-never-escalates property is kept UNWEAKENED; kill threshold 90 is far above the benign band). NFR35 (coverage: internal/response/fsm 84.8%, internal/response/netpol 85.5%, internal/config 84.1%, internal/response/override 75.3%, internal/response/audit 79.3%; no regression on Story 2.2/2.6/2.7/2.8). NFR42 (this matrix row + provenance).
- **ADRs:** none (the thirteen binding interpretations BI-1..BI-13 are recorded in the story artefact `4-1-preserved-killed-fsm-state-extension.md`).
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** FSM state-machine logic; the empirical evaluation harness is Epic 5 scope.

### `c3.10.1-adrs-contribution` (Story 6.1)

- **Story:** 6.1 (Architectural Decision Records (ADRs) and contribution guidelines; Epic 6, Operability and Documentation). A docs-only story: it documents the load-bearing architectural decisions and the contribution conventions so a contributor or operator inheriting Olaitan has the rationale preserved. It touches NO Go file, NATS subject, Redis family, schema, prompt, or Helm chart.
- **Merge SHA:** TBD (PR open against `epic-6-staging`; back-filled by `chore: backfill Story 6.1 merge SHA in traceability matrix` after merge). Branch `epic-6/story-6-1-adrs-and-contribution-guidelines` off `epic-6-staging`.
- **Deliverables (per AC):** AC1 patched `docs/schema-versioning.md` with the dual-publish MAJOR migration policy (7-day window) subsection (the schema-on-read MINOR rule and the per-row affected-subject references were already present; this filled the one confirmed gap, framed as an ADR). AC2 verified `docs/deferred-decisions.md` already consolidates the three spike outcomes (Sigma parser strategy `ADR-2026-04-28-01`, Calico flow-record export `ADR-2026-04-30-01`, CRIU forensic checkpoint `ADR-2026-05-02-01`) with the chosen path, rejected alternatives, and the linkage to the affected stories 1.10, 1.16, and 4.2 (all three links already present; no content patch required). AC3 created `docs/patterns.md` documenting seven patterns grounded in real source (NATS-subject and Redis-key constants, the self-describing envelope wrapper, the transient/permanent error taxonomy, the redaction reuse contract, idempotency keys, structured logging, and the single-goroutine-per-source model). AC4 created `docs/contributing.md` documenting the build/test/lint targets, the `traceability_updated` field, the golangci-lint expectations, the test-fixture conventions, and the forbidden patterns. AC5 created `docs/README.md` as the in-repo ADR index linking all four ADRs and the operator docs.
- **AC5 architecture-link interpretation (recorded decision, not a silent deviation):** the AC asks that each ADR be "linked from the architecture document". The canonical `architecture.md` lives in the planning workspace, NOT in this code repository, so it cannot be the in-repo link source and is not version-controlled here. The pragmatic resolution is `docs/README.md`: an in-repo ADR index that links all four ADRs and records that the canonical architecture document is the planning-workspace `architecture.md` (which the in-repo docs already reference by `architecture.md:NNN` section anchors). The index is the in-repo link surface the architecture document's design-level decisions point back to.
- **FRs/NFRs:** NFR34 (operator/contributor documentation: the ADRs and the contributing guide make the system self-service for an outsider). NFR42 (this matrix row plus provenance; the traceability self-reference).
- **ADRs:** the story patches/creates the ADR documents themselves; no NEW deferred decision is recorded (the AC5 architecture-link interpretation is recorded above and in the story Dev Notes).
- **test_files / test_ids rationale:** `n/a`. This is a prose-documentation story with no FR/NFR behaviour to exercise in a Go or Python test; the relevant gates are the docs-rendering review and the NFR42 traceability gate itself. `go build ./... && go vet ./...` were run to confirm the docs-only diff does not affect the Go build.
- **eval_run_ids rationale:** `n/a`; documentation, not evaluation-bearing.

## Conventions

- **`claim_id` grammar.** `c<chapter>.<section>[.<sub>]-<short-slug>`. The slug
  is lowercase, ASCII, hyphen-separated, and short (target ≤30 characters).
  It identifies the claim, not the implementing artefact, so a refactor that
  moves a package does **not** churn the `claim_id` (only `code_package`
  changes). Slugs are write-once and never renamed; if a claim is retired,
  the row is annotated and kept for audit, not deleted.
- **`eval_run_ids: n/a`.** Used for substrate (Helm chart, NATS subjects,
  Redis schema), infrastructure (CI gates, build pipelines), and feasibility
  spikes that pre-date the Epic 5 evaluation harness. Once the harness lands
  (Story 5.1), every row whose claim has empirical numbers in Chapter 4
  must back-fill this column with the canonical run identifier(s).
- **One ADR per row when one applies.** ADRs are recorded in
  `docs/deferred-decisions.md`. The matrix references them by ID
  (`YYYY-MM-DD-NN`) in the *Provenance* annex above. Multiple ADRs are
  comma-separated.
- **Sorting.** Rows are sorted by `claim_id` lexicographically. This makes
  the table byte-stable across re-renders and gives a natural §-order layout
  because the chapter prefix sorts first.
- **Provenance discipline.** Every new row added by a PR adds a
  *Provenance* sub-section (### `claim_id`) carrying the PR number, merge
  SHA, FRs/NFRs satisfied or informed, and any ADRs. The `traceability` CI
  gate does not parse this annex; it is for humans, but it is required by
  AC4 of the bootstrap story (Story 1.5) for every row.
- **Forward reference to NFR34.** Per NFR34, the operator runbook
  (`docs/runbook.md`, owned by Story 6.8) must include a sample matrix row
  in its on-boarding section so an operator copying the runbook can see the
  claim chain in situ. This file holds that obligation in trust until the
  runbook lands.
